# Goal-loop + dual-worktree pipeline (Layers 2+3, v1 plan)

Status: APPROVED — Gate 1 passed at iteration 4 of 5 (2026-07-18,
codex/gpt-5.5 @ xhigh, `degraded: model-fallback`).

## Context

baton v1 (Layer 1, cross-harness usage-limit failover) is complete: Gate-2
passed, a 40-finding surface audit folded, and the live e2e handoff loop
verified through the real installed plugin. Layers 2 and 3 automate the
development methodology itself on top of that foundation: a goal-driven loop
with review gates (Layer 2) and a dual-worktree adversarial write/review
pipeline (Layer 3).

## Locked decisions (Saksham, 2026-07-18)

1. **Packaging**: inside the baton repo — new commands `baton loop` and
   `baton pipeline`, sharing the role matrix, resolver, doctor, and
   checkpoint/handoff machinery.
2. **Layer 3 swap cadence**: per subtask — writer and reviewer swap seats
   every subtask; the planner role plans and the merger role performs the
   final adversarial merge (concrete chains live in `baton.config.json`).
3. **Layer 2 smoke gate**: one staged gate after planning — the loop must
   produce a minimal end-to-end slice (build + run + one real assertion) and
   get explicit approval before the full run; the 5-iteration cap governs
   every gate.
4. **Driver**: a supervisor CLI — spawns headless role children
   (`claude -p` / `codex exec`), parses verdicts, and invokes baton handoff
   automatically on limit death. Survives the operator's session ending.

## Design

### Loop spec

`loop.json` at repo root (schema `baton/loop@1`), scaffolded by
`baton loop init "<goal>"`:

- `goal`, `constraints[]` (falsifiable acceptance criteria — the loop's exit
  condition), `smoke` ({cmd, expect} — the staged-gate slice definition),
- `phases` — an ordered list of `{id, role}` entries: `id` is a free label
  for progress display; `role` MUST be a matrix role ID (validation fails on
  any unknown role before a child spawns). Default:
  plan(`planner`) → gate-1(`plan-reviewer`) → smoke — itself a TDD mini-cycle
  per the repo contract: smoke-tests(`test-author`) →
  smoke-verify(`test-verifier`) → smoke-build(`implementer`) → the human
  smoke gate — → per-subtask cycle (`test-author` → `test-verifier` →
  `implementer` → `subtask-reviewer`) → gate-2(`final-reviewer-a`,
  `final-reviewer-b`) → done,
- `budgets` ({iterationCap: 5, perRoleTimeoutMin, maxChildrenPerPhase}),
- roles resolve through the existing `baton.config.json` matrix — never
  hardcoded models.

### Role-matrix additions (Gate-1 iteration 1, finding 1)

The loop references roles by ID only; every referenced role must exist in the
matrix or `loop.json` validation fails (`unknown role '<id>'`, exit 2, before
any child spawns). Four roles are ADDED to `baton.config.json` +
`templates/baton.config.json.tpl` in the first implementation task:

- `subtask-reviewer` — per-subtask diff review inside the loop cycle.
- `merger` — the only role that writes main in pipeline mode; adversarial
  re-check before merge.
- `worker-a`, `worker-b` — the two pipeline seats. Subtask N assigns
  writer = seat (N mod 2), reviewer = the other seat; the SEATS swap, and each
  seat's concrete platform/model comes from its configured chain via the
  resolver. No vendor or model name appears in this plan, child prompts, or
  loop code — chains live in config only.

### `baton loop` (Layer 2)

- `baton loop init <goal>` — scaffold spec + plan template; idempotent,
  dry-run-able, same two-phase plan/apply discipline as `baton init`.
- `baton loop run [--approve-smoke <token>]` — the state machine. State lives
  in gitignored `.handoff/loop/state.json` + `journal.ndjson` (same atomic
  write + journal replay machinery as the bundle; loop position is also
  checkpointed into the handoff bundle so a limit death carries it).

### Root + ownership discipline (Gate-1 iteration 1, finding 2)

One authoritative root: the MAIN repo root where `loop.json` lives. The
supervisor is the SOLE writer of `.handoff/loop/` and of the main root's
handoff bundle; every supervisor-driven baton invocation passes explicit
`--root <mainRoot>` and a supervisor-owned stable session hint
(`--session loop-<runId>` — a NEW flag added to `checkpoint`; `receive`
already has it), so the bundle has exactly one stable owner.

**Child-hook policy (Gate-1 iteration 2, finding 1; iteration 3,
finding 1)** — children never touch OR read any bundle, by construction: the
supervisor sets `BATON_SUPERVISED_CHILD=1` in every child's environment, and
EVERY baton hook command — the hook entrypoints, `baton checkpoint`, and
`baton session-start` (which would otherwise read the bundle and inject
pending-handoff resume context into the child) — quietly no-ops (exit 0,
empty output) when it is set. This covers
Layer-2 children running in the MAIN root (where root discovery would
otherwise find the supervisor-owned bundle and the foreign-session guard
would reject their stable hints) and pipeline children in worktrees alike;
narrative checkpointing for supervised work is the supervisor's job, driven
by parsed child results. Contract tests: (a) Layer-2 — two concurrent
main-root children with distinct stable session hints and live hooks →
zero bundle mutations, zero foreign-session rejections, supervisor
`--session` checkpoints land; (b) pipeline — same from two worktrees while
the supervisor writes loop state.
- Each role invocation is a headless child under the supervisor:
  - claude-code roles: `claude -p "<role prompt>" --permission-mode
    acceptEdits --allowedTools ...` (allowlist per role: reviewers read-only).
  - codex roles: `codex exec -C <root> -s <sandbox> --model <m> -c
    model_reasoning_effort=<e> "<prompt>" </dev/null` (stdin closed — the
    stdin-hang class is a known field failure).
  - Verdict contract: children end with the structured tail
    (`VERDICT: APPROVED|APPROVED_WITH_NOTES|BLOCKED` + FINDINGS) already used
    by every review gate; the supervisor parses exactly that.

### Child supervision contract (Gate-1 iteration 1, finding 7)

`children.mjs` owns the full child lifecycle, not just argv assembly:

- Spawn detached in its OWN process group; stdin closed at spawn (the
  codex-exec stdin-hang class); per-role timeout → SIGTERM to the group, 10 s
  grace, then SIGKILL to the group (no zombie grandchildren; reap verified in
  tests with a child that forks a sleeper).
- Full child output streams to `.handoff/loop/children/<child-id>.log` with a
  byte cap (head preserved, middle truncated with a marker — the verdict tail
  is at the END so the tail is never truncated).
- **Verdict parsing is region-bounded (prompt-echo defense)**: only the
  child's FINAL message region is parsed — claude-code children run with a
  structured output format and the parser reads the result field; codex
  children are parsed strictly AFTER the last `tokens used` marker. The
  prompt region (which quotes `VERDICT:` in its instructions) is never
  scanned. Multiple tails in the final region → the LAST one wins.
- An unparseable, absent, or truncated verdict is BLOCKED-with-escalation,
  never APPROVED; a child that exits after making edits but before a verdict
  parks the subtask with its worktree preserved for inspection.
- A generic nonzero exit classifies as `other-error` (existing classifier
  rule) → the loop retries once, then parks; it NEVER triggers limit failover
  without limit-specific evidence.
- Fixture-driven tests: prompt echo, multiple verdict tails, truncated
  transcript, timeout kill/reap, zombie grandchild, non-limit nonzero exit.

### Supervisor lifetime and recovery (Gate-1 iteration 2, finding 5)

"Survives the operator's session ending" is a defined mechanism, not a hope:

- `baton loop run` is FOREGROUND by default. `--detach` re-launches the
  supervisor in its own session (`setsid`), output to
  `.handoff/loop/supervisor.log`, and returns.
- A run lock `.handoff/loop/supervisor.lock` carries {pid, process
  start-time, host, runId} — the same provably-dead machinery as the
  Layer-1 bundle lock. A second `loop run` against a LIVE supervisor is
  refused; against a provably-dead one it recovers.
- The state machine is journal-replayable: recovery replays
  `journal.ndjson`, marks any in-flight child of the dead supervisor as
  interrupted (its process group is gone or killed on adoption), and either
  continues from the last completed step or parks with an escalation note —
  never re-runs a completed gate iteration.
- Acceptance adds a supervisor-death test: kill the supervisor (and its
  launching session) mid-subtask → a fresh `loop run` recovers via the
  journal and continues or parks; no orphaned children survive (process
  groups reaped on adoption).

- **Smoke gate (staleness-proof — Gate-1 iteration 1, finding 8; TDD order —
  iteration 2, finding 2)**: after gate-1, the smoke slice is built as a TDD
  mini-cycle (test-author writes the failing smoke assertion → test-verifier
  approves it → implementer writes only enough to pass it — the repo TDD
  contract applies to the slice, not just to subtasks); the supervisor then
  runs `smoke.cmd`, records output, and STOPS, writing
  `.handoff/loop/SMOKE-REVIEW.md` plus `smoke-approval.json` containing an
  approval token = digest over {loop-state digest, smoke.cmd, output digest,
  HEAD, content-sensitive git digest (existing snapshot machinery), child
  assignment}. Resuming requires `baton loop run --approve-smoke <token>`;
  the supervisor recomputes the digest first and REFUSES a drifted token
  (anything changed since the human looked) — it parks for a fresh smoke run
  instead. No full-run budget is spent before a valid approval.
- **Limit failover (the baton integration — Gate-1 iteration 1, finding 3)**:
  an exact transaction, not a loose chain. On child death: (1) reap the
  process group and freeze the transcript; (2) classify via `baton detect`;
  non-limit → the retry/park path above. On a limit classification:
  (3) checkpoint the loop position INTO THE BUNDLE (supervisor
  `checkpoint --session loop-<runId>`) so the seal carries it and the receipt
  token binds it; (4) seal via `finalize --reason-class usage-limit --root
  <mainRoot>` (an already-dead/unsealed bundle takes the degraded-open path
  receive already supports); (5) refresh probes; (6) `receive --prepare` then
  `--commit` back-to-back with IDENTICAL intake flags, the supervisor session
  hint, and the same explicit root, with NO intervening writes; (7) resolver
  output (with the dead entry avoided) picks the relaunch platform+model;
  (8) relaunch the child with the resume prompt. A stale-token rejection
  triggers ONE automatic re-prepare/commit retry; a second failure parks.
  Between prepare and commit the failover holds the loop transaction lock and
  writes NOTHING — `.handoff/loop/` writes are not token-bound inputs, so
  this is enforced by construction and ASSERTED in tests (fs spy: zero
  `.handoff/loop/` writes inside the window), not assumed. Drift-rejection
  tests inject the inputs the token actually binds: git change and probe
  refresh between prepare and commit → stale-token → one retry path. Plus:
  open-bundle (unsealed) limit death; the full seal→receive→relaunch happy
  path.
- **Model-level failover (Gate-1 iteration 1, finding 4)**: the resolver
  gains entry-level avoidance — `avoidEntries: [{platform, model}]` alongside
  the existing platform-level `avoid[]` — and the classifier gains a
  `model-unavailable` signature class (distinct from usage-limit; seeded with
  the verified account-tier rejection string). A model rejected at spawn adds
  its {platform, model} entry to avoidEntries and re-resolves: other models
  on the SAME platform remain selectable. Named test: first chain entry
  rejected as model-unavailable → the next entry on the same platform is
  selected, with no platform-wide avoidance.
  All platforms/entries exhausted → the loop parks with a resume-at hint
  instead of dying.
- **Caps**: every gate loops at most 5 iterations; on exhaustion the loop
  parks and escalates (writes `.handoff/loop/ESCALATION.md`, notifies) — never
  a 6th run.

### `baton pipeline` (Layer 3 — a loop preset)

- `baton pipeline run` = `loop run` with the dual-worktree subtask cycle:
  - Two persistent worktrees `.worktrees/wt-a`, `.worktrees/wt-b`
    (`git worktree add`); `pipeline init` ensure-lines `.worktrees/` into
    `.gitignore` (same idempotent machinery as the `.handoff/` line).
  - Subtask N: the writer seat implements on a namespaced branch
    (`baton/wt-<seat>/subtask-N`) in its worktree; the reviewer seat reviews
    the diff from the OTHER worktree (fresh checkout, fresh context). Seats
    swap every subtask: worker-a writes / worker-b reviews → worker-b writes /
    worker-a reviews → … (concrete models per seat come from the role
    matrix — see Role-matrix additions).
  - Verdict flow: APPROVED → the merger role adversarially re-checks and
    merges into main (the ONLY writer to main); BLOCKED → writer fixes,
    ≤5 iterations, then escalate. After each merge both worktrees
    fast-forward.

  **Worktree transaction layer (Gate-1 iteration 1, finding 5)** —
  `worktrees.mjs` treats every git operation as a guarded transaction:
  - Branch namespace `baton/wt-*/…` is loop-owned; anything else is never
    touched.
  - Preflight before each child: worktree exists in `git worktree list`,
    index clean, HEAD on the expected branch — a dirty or drifted worktree
    parks the subtask instead of spawning.
  - Postflight after each child: HEAD still on the expected branch, main
    unmoved by the child — violation parks with the evidence.
  - Merges run under a supervisor-held merge lock; worktree sync after merge
    is ff-only (a non-ff sync is a corruption signal → park); merge conflicts
    park + escalate, never auto-resolved.
  - Stale metadata self-heals: a raw-deleted worktree directory is detected
    (`git worktree list` vs fs), pruned (`git worktree prune`), and re-added;
    teardown uses `git worktree remove` + branch deletion so acceptance
    constraint 5 (no residue) holds.
  - Adversarial tests: stale branch from a previous run, dirty worker,
    main moved mid-subtask, merge conflict, raw-deleted `.worktrees/`,
    untracked `.worktrees/` (missing gitignore line → doctor check).

  **Attribution enforcement for child commits (Gate-1 iteration 1,
  finding 6)** — the invariant is enforced, not assumed:
  - The supervisor sets `GIT_AUTHOR_NAME/EMAIL` and `GIT_COMMITTER_NAME/EMAIL`
    (sole author identity) in EVERY child's environment.
  - Every merge is gated on an attribution scan over the exact range
    `main..baton/wt-<seat>/subtask-N` — author/committer identity plus the
    forbidden-trailer patterns (the existing doctor patterns, applied to the
    full range, not a last-50 window). A violation blocks the merge and
    parks.
  - The existing doctor 50-commit scan remains the repo-wide backstop.

### Module layout (all under `core/src/loop/`)

`spec.mjs` (load/validate loop.json incl. role-existence check) · `state.mjs`

     1	/**
     2	 * Loop spec (baton/loop@1) — pure load/validate for the Layer-2 goal loop.
     3	 * The spec is committed project config (loop.json at the repo root); run
     4	 * state lives elsewhere (.handoff/loop/). Plan:
     5	 * docs/plans/2026-07-18-goal-loop-worktree-pipeline.md §"Loop spec".

     7	
     8	export const LOOP_SCHEMA = 'baton/loop@1';
     9	
    10	// The 5-cap is a repo-wide hard invariant (AGENTS.md §Review gates): a spec
    11	// asking for a 6th gate iteration is invalid, not a preference.
    12	export const ITERATION_CAP_MAX = 5;
    13	
    14	const DEFAULT_BUDGETS = Object.freeze({ iterationCap: 5, perRoleTimeoutMin: 30, maxChildrenPerPhase: 10 });
    15	
    16	/**
    17	 * The scaffold `baton loop init` writes. Phase ids are progress labels;
    18	 * every role is a matrix role ID resolved via baton.config.json — never a
    19	 * model name.
    20	 * @param {string} goal
    21	 * @returns {any}
    22	 */
    23	export function defaultLoopSpec(goal) {
    24	  return {
    25	    schema: LOOP_SCHEMA,
    26	    goal,
    27	    constraints: [],
    28	    smoke: { cmd: null, expect: null },
    29	    phases: [
    30	      { id: 'plan', role: 'planner' },
    31	      { id: 'gate-1', role: 'plan-reviewer' },
    32	      // The smoke slice is itself a TDD mini-cycle (Gate-1 iter-2 finding 2):
    33	      // failing smoke assertion → verifier approval → minimal implementation.
    34	      { id: 'smoke-tests', role: 'test-author' },
    35	      { id: 'smoke-verify', role: 'test-verifier' },
    36	      { id: 'smoke-build', role: 'implementer' },
    37	      { id: 'subtask-tests', role: 'test-author' },
    38	      { id: 'subtask-verify', role: 'test-verifier' },
    39	      { id: 'subtask-implement', role: 'implementer' },
    40	      { id: 'subtask-review', role: 'subtask-reviewer' },
    41	      { id: 'gate-2-a', role: 'final-reviewer-a' },
    42	      { id: 'gate-2-b', role: 'final-reviewer-b' },
    43	    ],
    44	    budgets: { ...DEFAULT_BUDGETS },
    45	  };
    46	}
    47	
    48	/**
    49	 * Validate a loop spec against the role matrix. Reports EVERY problem at
    50	 * once (each error names its offender) so a spec author fixes one round, not
    51	 * one field per run. Valid specs are returned normalized: caller-provided
    52	 * fields are preserved verbatim; only ABSENT constraints/budgets/smoke are
    53	 * filled with defaults.
    54	 * @param {any} spec
    55	 * @param {any} config the parsed baton.config.json (roles are checked against it)
    56	 * @returns {{ok: true, spec: any} | {ok: false, errors: string[]}}
    57	 */
    58	export function validateLoopSpec(spec, config) {
    59	  /** @type {string[]} */
    60	  const errors = [];
    61	  const s = spec ?? {};
    62	
    63	  if (s.schema !== LOOP_SCHEMA) errors.push(`schema must be '${LOOP_SCHEMA}' (got ${JSON.stringify(s.schema)})`);
    64	  if (typeof s.goal !== 'string' || s.goal.length === 0) errors.push('goal must be a non-empty string');
    65	
    66	  let constraints = s.constraints;
    67	  if (constraints === undefined) constraints = [];
    68	  if (!Array.isArray(constraints)) {
    69	    errors.push('constraints must be an array of strings');
    70	  } else {
    71	    constraints.forEach((c, i) => {
    72	      if (typeof c !== 'string') errors.push(`constraints[${i}] must be a string (got ${JSON.stringify(c)})`);
    73	    });
    74	  }
    75	
    76	  const roles = config?.roles ?? {};
    77	  if (!Array.isArray(s.phases) || s.phases.length === 0) {
    78	    errors.push('phases must be a non-empty array of {id, role}');
    79	  } else {
    80	    s.phases.forEach((/** @type {any} */ p, /** @type {number} */ i) => {
    81	      if (typeof p?.id !== 'string' || p.id.length === 0) errors.push(`phases[${i}] is missing an id (a non-empty progress label)`);
    82	      if (typeof p?.role !== 'string' || p.role.length === 0) {
    83	        errors.push(`phases[${i}] is missing a role (a baton.config.json role ID)`);
    84	      } else if (!(p.role in roles)) {
    85	        errors.push(`phases[${i}]: unknown role '${p.role}' — add it to baton.config.json roles or fix the reference`);
    86	      }
    87	    });
    88	  }
    89	
    90	  let budgets = s.budgets;
    91	  if (budgets === undefined) {
    92	    budgets = { ...DEFAULT_BUDGETS };
    93	  } else if (typeof budgets !== 'object' || budgets === null) {
    94	    errors.push('budgets must be an object');
    95	  } else {
    96	    const cap = budgets.iterationCap;
    97	    if (typeof cap !== 'number' || !Number.isFinite(cap) || cap < 1 || cap > ITERATION_CAP_MAX) {
    98	      errors.push(`budgets.iterationCap must be a number between 1 and ${ITERATION_CAP_MAX} (the 5-iteration cap is a hard invariant; got ${JSON.stringify(cap)})`);
    99	    }
   100	    for (const field of ['perRoleTimeoutMin', 'maxChildrenPerPhase']) {
   101	      const v = budgets[field];
   102	      if (typeof v !== 'number' || !Number.isFinite(v) || v <= 0) {
   103	        errors.push(`budgets.${field} must be a positive number (got ${JSON.stringify(v)})`);
   104	      }
   105	    }
   106	  }
   107	
   108	  let smoke = s.smoke;
   109	  if (smoke === undefined) {
   110	    smoke = { cmd: null, expect: null };
   111	  } else if (typeof smoke !== 'object' || smoke === null) {
   112	    errors.push('smoke must be an object ({cmd, expect}, each a string or null)');
   113	  } else {
   114	    if (!(smoke.cmd === null || typeof smoke.cmd === 'string')) errors.push(`smoke.cmd must be a string or null (got ${JSON.stringify(smoke.cmd)})`);
   115	    if (!(smoke.expect === null || typeof smoke.expect === 'string')) errors.push(`smoke.expect must be a string or null (got ${JSON.stringify(smoke.expect)})`);
   116	  }
   117	
   118	  if (errors.length > 0) return { ok: false, errors };
   119	  return { ok: true, spec: { ...s, constraints, budgets, smoke } };
   120	}

 succeeded in 0ms:
     1	/**
     2	 * Loop run state — snapshot + journal + replay for the Layer-2 supervisor,
     3	 * mirroring the bundle store's crash-tolerance idioms (atomic tmp+rename
     4	 * snapshots, torn-tail-tolerant NDJSON, seq-dedup replay). Everything lives
     5	 * under <root>/.handoff/loop/; the supervisor is the sole writer. Plan:
     6	 * docs/plans/2026-07-18-goal-loop-worktree-pipeline.md §"baton loop",
     7	 * §"Smoke gate", §"Supervisor lifetime and recovery", §"Caps".
     8	 */
     9	import { appendEntry, readAllTolerant } from '../util/jsonl.mjs';
    10	import { atomicWriteJson, safeReadJson, ensureDir } from '../util/fsx.mjs';
    11	import { dedupeKey } from '../util/ids.mjs';
    12	
    13	export const LOOP_STATE_SCHEMA = 'baton/loop-state@1';
    14	
    15	/** The frozen status vocabulary. */
    16	export const LOOP_STATUS = Object.freeze({
    17	  RUNNING: 'running',
    18	  AWAITING_SMOKE_APPROVAL: 'awaiting-smoke-approval',
    19	  PARKED: 'parked',
    20	  ESCALATED: 'escalated',
    21	  DONE: 'done',
    22	});
    23	
    24	/** The frozen journal event vocabulary. */
    25	export const LOOP_EVENT = Object.freeze({
    26	  PHASE_ADVANCE: 'phase-advance',
    27	  GATE_ITERATION: 'gate-iteration',
    28	  PARK: 'park',
    29	  RESUME: 'resume',
    30	  SMOKE_AWAIT: 'smoke-await',
    31	  SMOKE_APPROVE: 'smoke-approve',
    32	});
    33	
    34	// Every gate loops at most 5 times — a repo-wide hard invariant (AGENTS.md
    35	// §Review gates); the reducer refuses a 6th and escalates instead.
    36	const GATE_ITERATION_CAP = 5;
    37	
    38	/** @param {string} root */
    39	export function loopPaths(root) {
    40	  const dir = `${root}/.handoff/loop`;
    41	  return { dir, state: `${dir}/state.json`, journal: `${dir}/journal.ndjson` };
    42	}
    43	
    44	/**
    45	 * Fresh run state — pure and deterministic from the injected io (identity
    46	 * facts + clock only; no Date.now/Math.random, which would break replay).
    47	 * @param {any} spec a VALIDATED loop spec @param {any} io
    48	 */
    49	export function initLoopState(spec, io) {
    50	  const runId = `loop-${dedupeKey({ host: io.host, pid: io.pid, startTime: io.startTime, at: io.now() }).slice(0, 12)}`;
    51	  /** @type {Record<string, number>} */
    52	  const iterations = {};
    53	  for (const p of spec.phases ?? []) {
    54	    if (/gate/.test(String(p.id))) iterations[p.id] = 0;
    55	  }
    56	  return {
    57	    schema: LOOP_STATE_SCHEMA,
    58	    runId,
    59	    goal: spec.goal,
    60	    phaseCount: Array.isArray(spec.phases) ? spec.phases.length : 0,
    61	    phaseIndex: 0,
    62	    iterations,
    63	    status: LOOP_STATUS.RUNNING,
    64	    parkReason: null,
    65	    escalation: null,
    66	    smokeApproval: null,
    67	    createdAt: io.now(),
    68	    journalSeq: 0,
    69	  };
    70	}
    71	
    72	/**
    73	 * The pure transition reducer. Never mutates its input; unknown events and
    74	 * refused transitions return the input state unchanged (a new reference is
    75	 * only produced for a real transition).
    76	 * @param {any} state @param {any} ev
    77	 */
    78	export function applyLoopEvent(state, ev) {
    79	  switch (ev?.type) {
    80	    case LOOP_EVENT.PHASE_ADVANCE: {
    81	      const next = state.phaseIndex + 1;
    82	      if (next >= state.phaseCount) return { ...state, phaseIndex: state.phaseCount, status: LOOP_STATUS.DONE };
    83	      return { ...state, phaseIndex: next };
    84	    }
    85	    case LOOP_EVENT.GATE_ITERATION: {
    86	      const gate = String(ev.gate);
    87	      const current = state.iterations?.[gate] ?? 0;
    88	      // The 6th attempt is REFUSED: the counter stays at the cap and the run
    89	      // escalates with a record naming the gate — never a 6th run.
    90	      if (current >= GATE_ITERATION_CAP) {
    91	        return { ...state, status: LOOP_STATUS.ESCALATED, escalation: { gate, iteration: current } };
    92	      }
    93	      return { ...state, iterations: { ...state.iterations, [gate]: current + 1 } };
    94	    }
    95	    case LOOP_EVENT.PARK:
    96	      return { ...state, status: LOOP_STATUS.PARKED, parkReason: ev.reason ?? null };
    97	    case LOOP_EVENT.RESUME:
    98	      // Only a PARK is resumable; an escalation is an operator decision.
    99	      if (state.status !== LOOP_STATUS.PARKED) return state;
   100	      return { ...state, status: LOOP_STATUS.RUNNING, parkReason: null };
   101	    case LOOP_EVENT.SMOKE_AWAIT:
   102	      return { ...state, status: LOOP_STATUS.AWAITING_SMOKE_APPROVAL };
   103	    case LOOP_EVENT.SMOKE_APPROVE: {
   104	      // Guarded transition: only meaningful while awaiting, and only with the
   105	      // caller's verifySmokeToken result stamped verified === true. A missing
   106	      // or false `verified` is unverified — it never resumes the run.
   107	      if (state.status !== LOOP_STATUS.AWAITING_SMOKE_APPROVAL) return state;
   108	      if (ev.verified !== true) return state;
   109	      return { ...state, status: LOOP_STATUS.RUNNING, smokeApproval: { token: ev.token, at: ev.ts ?? null } };
   110	    }
   111	    default:
   112	      return state;
   113	  }
   114	}
   115	
   116	/**
   117	 * Atomic snapshot write (tmp + rename under .handoff/loop).
   118	 * @param {string} root @param {any} state @param {any} io
   119	 */
   120	export async function writeLoopState(root, state, io) {
   121	  const p = loopPaths(root);
   122	  ensureDir(io.fs, p.dir);
   123	  atomicWriteJson(io.fs, p.state, state);
   124	}
   125	
   126	/**
   127	 * Append one journal event; allocates the next seq past
   128	 * max(snapshot.journalSeq, journal tail) unless the entry carries one.
   129	 * @param {string} root @param {any} ev @param {any} io
   130	 * @returns {Promise<number>} the persisted seq
   131	 */
   132	export async function appendLoopEvent(root, ev, io) {
   133	  const p = loopPaths(root);
   134	  ensureDir(io.fs, p.dir);
   135	  let seq = ev.seq;
   136	  if (typeof seq !== 'number') {
   137	    const snap = safeReadJson(io.fs, p.state);
   138	    const snapSeq = snap.ok && typeof snap.value?.journalSeq === 'number' ? snap.value.journalSeq : 0;
   139	    let tailSeq = 0;
   140	    if (io.fs.existsSync(p.journal)) {
   141	      for (const e of readAllTolerant(io.fs, p.journal).entries) {
   142	        if (typeof e.seq === 'number' && e.seq > tailSeq) tailSeq = e.seq;
   143	      }
   144	    }
   145	    seq = Math.max(snapSeq, tailSeq) + 1;
   146	  }
   147	  const ts = typeof ev.ts === 'string' ? ev.ts : io.now();
   148	  const entry = {
   149	    ...ev,
   150	    seq,
   151	    ts,
   152	    dedupeKey: typeof ev.dedupeKey === 'string' && ev.dedupeKey.length > 0 ? ev.dedupeKey : dedupeKey({ seq, ts, type: ev.type, gate: ev.gate ?? null }),
   153	  };
   154	  appendEntry(io.fs, p.journal, entry);
   155	  return seq;
   156	}
   157	
   158	/**
   159	 * Load = snapshot + torn-tail-tolerant replay of journal events PAST the
   160	 * snapshot's seq. Duplicate seqs/dedupeKeys apply exactly once, so a
   161	 * supervisor-death recovery never re-runs a completed iteration.
   162	 * @param {string} root @param {any} io
   163	 * @returns {Promise<{state: any, warnings: string[]}>}
   164	 */
   165	export async function loadLoopState(root, io) {
   166	  const p = loopPaths(root);
   167	  /** @type {string[]} */
   168	  const warnings = [];
   169	  const snap = safeReadJson(io.fs, p.state);
   170	  if (!snap.ok) return { state: null, warnings: ['no loop state snapshot found'] };
   171	  let state = snap.value;
   172	  const baseSeq = typeof state.journalSeq === 'number' ? state.journalSeq : 0;
   173	
   174	  if (io.fs.existsSync(p.journal)) {
   175	    const { entries, warnings: jw } = readAllTolerant(io.fs, p.journal);
   176	    for (const w of jw ?? []) warnings.push(`journal: ${w.kind ?? 'warning'} at line ${w.line ?? '?'}`);
   177	    const applied = new Set();
   178	    let maxSeq = baseSeq;
   179	    for (const e of entries) {
   180	      const seq = typeof e.seq === 'number' ? e.seq : null;
   181	      if (seq !== null && seq <= baseSeq) continue; // already folded into the snapshot
   182	      const key = e.dedupeKey ?? (seq !== null ? `seq:${seq}` : null);
   183	      if (key !== null && applied.has(key)) continue; // replayed duplicate — apply once
   184	      if (seq !== null && applied.has(`seq:${seq}`)) continue;
   185	      state = applyLoopEvent(state, e);
   186	      if (key !== null) applied.add(key);
   187	      if (seq !== null) {
   188	        applied.add(`seq:${seq}`);
   189	        if (seq > maxSeq) maxSeq = seq;
   190	      }
   191	    }
   192	    state = { ...state, journalSeq: maxSeq };
   193	  }
   194	  return { state, warnings };
   195	}
   196	
   197	// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   198	// Smoke-approval token — a STRUCTURED digest over the six bound inputs, so a
   199	// stale approval is refused NAMING what moved (mirrors the receive receipt
   200	// token's driftedInputs discipline).
   201	// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   202	
   203	const SMOKE_TOKEN_PREFIX = 'smk1';
   204	const SMOKE_TOKEN_FIELDS = /** @type {const} */ ([
   205	  'stateDigest',
   206	  'smokeCmd',
   207	  'smokeOutputDigest',
   208	  'gitHead',
   209	  'gitContentDigest',
   210	  'childAssignment',
   211	]);
   212	
   213	/**
   214	 * Deterministic structured token. Per-field digests keep field boundaries
   215	 * (no concatenation collisions) and let verification name drifted inputs.
   216	 * Missing bound fields throw — a token over partial inputs is meaningless.
   217	 * @param {Record<string, string>} inputs
   218	 * @returns {string}
   219	 */
   220	export function smokeApprovalToken(inputs) {
   221	  for (const f of SMOKE_TOKEN_FIELDS) {
   222	    if (typeof inputs?.[f] !== 'string') throw new Error(`smoke approval token: required input '${f}' is missing`);
   223	  }
   224	  return `${SMOKE_TOKEN_PREFIX}.` + SMOKE_TOKEN_FIELDS.map((f) => dedupeKey(inputs[f]).slice(0, 10)).join('.');
   225	}
   226	
   227	/**
   228	 * Recompute against current inputs; positional comparison names every
   229	 * drifted input. Never throws — partial current inputs simply cannot verify.
   230	 * @param {string} presented @param {Record<string, string>} inputs
   231	 * @returns {{ok: true} | {ok: false, driftedInputs: string[]}}
   232	 */
   233	export function verifySmokeToken(presented, inputs) {
   234	  /** @type {string} */
   235	  let current;
   236	  try {
   237	    current = smokeApprovalToken(inputs);
   238	  } catch {
   239	    return { ok: false, driftedInputs: SMOKE_TOKEN_FIELDS.filter((f) => typeof inputs?.[f] !== 'string') };
   240	  }
   241	  if (presented === current) return { ok: true };
   242	  const a = String(presented ?? '').split('.');
   243	  const b = current.split('.');
   244	  /** @type {string[]} */
   245	  const drifted = [];
   246	  SMOKE_TOKEN_FIELDS.forEach((f, i) => {
   247	    if (a[i + 1] !== b[i + 1]) drifted.push(f);
   248	  });
   249	  return { ok: false, driftedInputs: drifted.length > 0 ? drifted : [...SMOKE_TOKEN_FIELDS] };
   250	}

     1	/**
     2	 * Child supervision — the loop's reliability surface. Owns the FULL child

    29	/**
    30	 * Assemble the spawn contract for a role child. Reviewers are read-only on
    31	 * every platform; every child is marked supervised (its own baton hooks
    32	 * no-op), carries the sole-author git identity, and has stdin CLOSED at
    33	 * spawn (the codex-exec stdin-hang field failure).
    34	 * @param {{platform: string, role: string, model: string, effort?: string | null, mode?: string}} assignment
    35	 * @param {string} prompt

    39	export function buildChildArgv(assignment, prompt, opts) {

    61	const VERDICT_RE = /VERDICT:\s*(APPROVED_WITH_NOTES|APPROVED|BLOCKED)\b/g;
    62	
    63	/** @param {string} region @returns {{verdict: string, findings: string} | null} */
    64	function lastVerdictIn(region) {
    65	  let last = null;
    66	  for (const m of region.matchAll(VERDICT_RE)) last = m;
    67	  if (!last) return null;
    68	  const after = region.slice((last.index ?? 0) + last[0].length);
    69	  return { verdict: last[1], findings: after.trim() };
    70	}
    71	
    72	/**
    73	 * Region-bounded verdict parsing — the prompt region (which quotes the
    74	 * verdict format in its instructions) is NEVER scanned, and there is no
    75	 * whole-transcript fallback. Unparseable is BLOCKED, never APPROVED.
    76	 * - codex: only the text after the LAST line starting with 'tokens used'.
    77	 * - claude-code: only the `result` field of the structured JSON output.
    78	 * Multiple tails inside the trusted region → the LAST one wins.
    79	 * @param {string} transcript @param {{platform: string}} opts
    80	 * @returns {{verdict: string, findings: string, reason?: string}}
    81	 */
    82	export function parseVerdict(transcript, { platform }) {
    83	  const unparseable = { verdict: 'BLOCKED', findings: '', reason: 'unparseable' };
    84	  const text = String(transcript ?? '');
    85	
    86	  if (platform === 'claude-code') {
    87	    let obj = null;
    88	    try {
    89	      obj = JSON.parse(text.trim());
    90	    } catch {
    91	      return unparseable;
    92	    }
    93	    if (typeof obj?.result !== 'string') return unparseable;
    94	    const hit = lastVerdictIn(obj.result);
    95	    return hit ?? unparseable;
    96	  }
    97	
    98	  // codex: locate the LAST 'tokens used' marker line; the trusted region is
    99	  // strictly after it.
   100	  const lines = text.split('\n');
   101	  let markerLine = -1;
   102	  for (let i = 0; i < lines.length; i += 1) {
   103	    if (/^tokens used\b/i.test(lines[i])) markerLine = i;
   104	  }
   105	  if (markerLine === -1) return unparseable;
   106	  const region = lines.slice(markerLine + 1).join('\n');
   107	  const hit = lastVerdictIn(region);
   108	  return hit ?? unparseable;
   109	}
   110	
   111	const TRUNCATION_MARKER = '\n[... truncated by baton log cap ...]\n';
   112	
   113	/**
   114	 * Cap a transcript: head preserved, middle replaced with a marker, TAIL
   115	 * preserved verbatim (the verdict lives at the end and must never be cut).
   116	 * @param {string} text @param {number} maxBytes
   117	 * @returns {string}
   118	 */
   119	export function capLog(text, maxBytes) {
   120	  const s = String(text ?? '');
   121	  if (Buffer.byteLength(s) <= maxBytes) return s;
   122	  const headBytes = Math.max(1, Math.floor(maxBytes * 0.4));
   123	  const tailBytes = Math.max(1, maxBytes - headBytes);
   124	  const buf = Buffer.from(s);
   125	  const head = buf.subarray(0, headBytes).toString();
   126	  const tail = buf.subarray(buf.length - tailBytes).toString();
   127	  return head + TRUNCATION_MARKER + tail;
   128	}
   129	
   130	/**
   131	 * Exit classification delegates to core detect — one classifier, one
   132	 * signature table, no drift. Returns exactly classify(...).class.
   133	 * @param {string} transcript @param {number} exitCode @param {string} platform @param {any} table
   134	 * @returns {string}
   135	 */
   136	export function classifyChildExit(transcript, exitCode, platform, table) {
   137	  return classify({ text: transcript, exitCode, platform, table }).class;
   138	}
   139	
   140	/** @param {number} pid @param {NodeJS.Signals} sig */
   141	function killGroup(pid, sig) {
   142	  try {
   143	    process.kill(-pid, sig);
   144	  } catch {
   145	    // ESRCH — already gone.
   146	  }
   147	}
   148	
   149	/**
   150	 * Spawn and supervise one child: detached in its OWN process group, stdin
   151	 * closed; on timeout SIGTERM the group, wait graceMs, SIGKILL the group (no
   152	 * zombie grandchildren). Output streams to a capped log at opts.logPath; the
   153	 * verdict is parsed from the full transcript, region-bounded.
   154	 * @param {{command: string, args: string[], env?: Record<string, string>, cwd?: string}} spec
   155	 * @param {{timeoutMs: number, graceMs: number, logPath: string, maxLogBytes?: number, platform?: string}} opts
   156	 * @returns {Promise<{timedOut: boolean, exitCode: number | null, verdict?: string, findings?: string, reason?: string, logPath: string}>}
   157	 */
   158	export function superviseChild(spec, opts) {
   159	  return new Promise((resolve) => {
   160	    const maxLogBytes = opts.maxLogBytes ?? 1_000_000;
   161	    const child = spawn(spec.command, spec.args, {
   162	      cwd: spec.cwd,
   163	      env: { ...process.env, ...(spec.env ?? {}) },
   164	      stdio: ['ignore', 'pipe', 'pipe'],
   165	      detached: true, // its own process group — the kill unit
   166	    });
   167	
   168	    let out = '';
   169	    let timedOut = false;
   170	    /** @type {NodeJS.Timeout | null} */
   171	    let killTimer = null;
   172	
   173	    const onChunk = (/** @type {Buffer} */ c) => {
   174	      out += c.toString();
   175	    };
   176	    child.stdout?.on('data', onChunk);
   177	    child.stderr?.on('data', onChunk);
   178	
   179	    const timeout = setTimeout(() => {
   180	      timedOut = true;
   181	      if (typeof child.pid === 'number') {
   182	        killGroup(child.pid, 'SIGTERM');
   183	        killTimer = setTimeout(() => {
   184	          if (typeof child.pid === 'number') killGroup(child.pid, 'SIGKILL');
   185	        }, opts.graceMs);
   186	      }
   187	    }, opts.timeoutMs);
   188	
   189	    const finish = (/** @type {number | null} */ exitCode) => {
   190	      clearTimeout(timeout);
   191	      if (killTimer) clearTimeout(killTimer);
   192	      // Belt-and-braces: reap any group survivors even on a clean exit path.
   193	      if (timedOut && typeof child.pid === 'number') killGroup(child.pid, 'SIGKILL');
   194	      try {
   195	        mkdirSync(dirname(opts.logPath), { recursive: true });
   196	        writeFileSync(opts.logPath, capLog(out, maxLogBytes));
   197	      } catch {
   198	        // The log must never mask the result.
   199	      }
   200	      const parsed = parseVerdict(out, { platform: opts.platform ?? 'codex' });
   201	      resolve({
   202	        timedOut,
   203	        exitCode: timedOut ? null : exitCode,
   204	        verdict: parsed.verdict,
   205	        findings: parsed.findings,
   206	        ...(parsed.reason ? { reason: parsed.reason } : {}),
   207	        logPath: opts.logPath,
   208	      });
   209	    };
   210	
   211	    child.on('error', () => finish(null));
   212	    child.on('close', (code) => finish(code));
   213	  });
   214	}

     1	/**
     2	 * Loop failover orchestration — what the supervisor does when a child dies.
     3	 * Classifies the death via the signature table, then takes exactly one path:
     4	 * retry-once-then-park (generic failures), entry-level re-resolve
     5	 * (model-unavailable — the platform survives its dead model), or the
     6	 * usage-limit transaction: checkpoint the loop position INTO the bundle,
     7	 * seal, and receive onto the resolver's pick with one bounded stale retry.
     8	 * Writes NOTHING under .handoff/loop/ — the supervisor applies the returned
     9	 * decision to loop state afterward. Plan:
    10	 * docs/plans/2026-07-18-goal-loop-worktree-pipeline.md §"Limit failover" +
    11	 * §"Model-level failover".
    12	 */
    13	import { fileURLToPath } from 'node:url';
    14	import { join, dirname } from 'node:path';
    15	import { loadSignatures } from '../detect/signatures.mjs';
    16	import { classify } from '../detect/classifier.mjs';
    17	import { resolveRoles } from '../roles/resolve.mjs';
    18	import { loadBundle } from '../bundle/store.mjs';
    19	import { snapshot as gitSnapshot } from '../git/snapshot.mjs';
    20	import { prepare, commit } from '../receive/txn.mjs';
    21	import { cmdCheckpoint } from '../commands/checkpoint.mjs';
    22	import { cmdFinalize } from '../commands/finalize.mjs';
    23	
    24	const BUILTIN_SIGNATURES = join(dirname(fileURLToPath(import.meta.url)), '..', '..', 'data', 'signatures.v1.json');
    25	
    26	/**
    27	 * @param {{
    28	 *   root: string, io: any, config: any, role: string,
    29	 *   assignment: {platform: string, role?: string, model: string, effort?: string | null, mode?: string},
    30	 *   transcript: string, exitCode: number, sessionHint: string,
    31	 *   probes?: any, avoid?: string[], avoidEntries?: Array<{platform: string, model: string}>,
    32	 *   attempt: number, loopState?: any, degradedOpen?: boolean,
    33	 * }} input
    34	 * @returns {Promise<
    35	 *   | {action: 'retry', class: string, attempt: number}
    36	 *   | {action: 'park', class: string, reason: string, resumeAt?: string}
    37	 *   | {action: 'relaunch', class: string, assignment: any, avoidEntries?: any[], prompt?: string}
    38	 * >}
    39	 */
    40	export async function runFailover(input) {
    41	  const { root, io, config, role, assignment, sessionHint } = input;
    42	  const avoid = input.avoid ?? [];
    43	  const avoidEntries = input.avoidEntries ?? [];
    44	
    45	  // Classification is TABLE-driven — the same loader/classifier as `baton
    46	  // detect`, so overlay/fixture signatures are honored, never hardcoded.
    47	  const table = loadSignatures({ builtinPath: BUILTIN_SIGNATURES }, io);
    48	  const verdict = classify({ text: input.transcript, exitCode: input.exitCode, platform: assignment.platform, table });
    49	
    50	  // Model-level failure: the platform is fine, one model is dead. Avoid the
    51	  // exact {platform, model} tuple and re-resolve — no seal, no receive.
    52	  if (verdict.class === 'model-unavailable') {
    53	    const nextAvoidEntries = [...avoidEntries, { platform: assignment.platform, model: assignment.model }];
    54	    const resolved = resolveRoles({ config, to: assignment.platform, avoid, avoidEntries: nextAvoidEntries, probes: input.probes ?? null });
    55	    const next = resolved.assignments[role];
    56	    if (!next || next.mode === 'unavailable') {
    57	      return { action: 'park', class: verdict.class, reason: `no eligible chain entry left for role '${role}' after avoiding ${assignment.platform}/${assignment.model}` };
    58	    }
    59	    return { action: 'relaunch', class: verdict.class, assignment: next, avoidEntries: nextAvoidEntries };
    60	  }
    61	
    62	  // Anything that is not a usage-limit death is a child failure, not a
    63	  // platform death: retry once, then park. Never a seal or receive.
    64	  if (verdict.class !== 'usage-limit') {
    65	    if (input.attempt <= 1) return { action: 'retry', class: verdict.class, attempt: input.attempt };
    66	    return { action: 'park', class: verdict.class, reason: `child failed twice (${verdict.class}) — parked for operator review` };
    67	  }
    68	
    69	  // ---- Usage-limit: the exact transaction (plan steps 3–8). ----------------
    70	  const { bundle } = loadBundle(root, io);
    71	  const originPlatform = bundle?.origin?.platform ?? assignment.platform;
    72	
    73	  // (3) Checkpoint the loop position INTO the bundle under the supervisor
    74	  // session BEFORE any seal, so the frozen seal carries it.
    75	  if (input.loopState !== undefined) {
    76	    const checkpointIo = {
    77	      ...io,
    78	      stdin: JSON.stringify({
    79	        schema: 'baton/event@1',
    80	        events: [{ type: 'decision', payload: { summary: `loop position at limit death: ${JSON.stringify(input.loopState)}` } }],
    81	      }),
    82	    };
    83	    await cmdCheckpoint(['--platform', originPlatform, '--session', sessionHint, '--root', root], checkpointIo);
    84	  }
    85	
    86	  // (4) Seal — unless the caller knows a clean seal is impossible
    87	  // (degradedOpen: a prior death left the bundle unsealed; receive's
    88	  // degraded-open path compensates), or the bundle is already sealed.
    89	  const alreadySealed = bundle?.handoff?.status === 'sealed';
    90	  if (input.degradedOpen !== true && !alreadySealed) {
    91	    await cmdFinalize(['--reason', 'usage-limit death during a supervised loop run', '--reason-class', 'usage-limit', '--root', root], io);
    92	  }
    93	
    94	  // (7, ordered before receive because receive needs the target) — resolver
    95	  // pick with the dead origin platform avoided.
    96	  const relaunchAvoid = avoid.includes(assignment.platform) ? avoid : [...avoid, assignment.platform];
    97	  const resolved = resolveRoles({ config, to: assignment.platform, avoid: relaunchAvoid, avoidEntries, probes: input.probes ?? null });
    98	  const target = resolved.assignments[role];
    99	  if (!target || target.mode === 'unavailable') {
   100	    return {
   101	      action: 'park',
   102	      class: verdict.class,
   103	      reason: `every platform is avoided or unavailable for role '${role}' — parked with a resume hint`,
   104	      ...(verdict.resetHint ? { resumeAt: verdict.resetHint } : {}),
   105	    };
   106	  }
   107	
   108	  // (5–6) Receive prepare+commit back-to-back with IDENTICAL intake and the
   109	  // supervisor session hint. Git is re-derived fresh for each phase (exactly
   110	  // as the CLI does), so real drift inside the window stales the token; ONE
   111	  // automatic re-prepare retry, then park. No .handoff/loop writes anywhere.
   112	  const receiveOpts = (/** @type {any} */ git) => ({
   113	    platform: target.platform,
   114	    origin: 'unknown', // defaults from the sealed bundle's recorded origin
   115	    reason: 'unspecified', // defaults from the sealed reason
   116	    reasonClass: 'usage-limit',
   117	    gitSnapshot: git,
   118	    probes: input.probes ?? null,
   119	    sessionHint,
   120	    sessionUnstable: false,
   121	  });
   122	
   123	  /** @type {string} */
   124	  let prompt = '';
   125	  let lastError = null;
   126	  for (let window = 1; window <= 2; window += 1) {
   127	    const prepGit = await gitSnapshot({ execFile: io.execFile, cwd: root, fs: io.fs });
   128	    const prepared = prepare(root, receiveOpts(prepGit), io);
   129	    prompt = prepared.prompt;
   130	    const commitGit = await gitSnapshot({ execFile: io.execFile, cwd: root, fs: io.fs });
   131	    try {
   132	      commit(root, prepared.token, receiveOpts(commitGit), io);
   133	      lastError = null;
   134	      break;
   135	    } catch (err) {
   136	      lastError = err;
   137	    }
   138	  }
   139	  if (lastError !== null) {
   140	    const msg = /** @type {any} */ (lastError)?.message ?? String(lastError);
   141	    return { action: 'park', class: verdict.class, reason: `receive commit stayed stale after one re-prepare retry — ${msg}` };
   142	  }
   143	
   144	  // (8) Relaunch on the adopted platform with the resume prompt.
   145	  return {
   146	    action: 'relaunch',
   147	    class: verdict.class,
   148	    assignment: target,
   149	    avoidEntries,
   150	    prompt,
   151	  };
   152	}

    46	/** Parse `git worktree list --porcelain` into [{path, branch}]. @param {string} out */
    47	function parseWorktreeList(out) {
    48	  /** @type {Array<{path: string, branch: string | null}>} */
    49	  const entries = [];

    59	/**
    60	 * Ensure both seat worktrees exist on their namespaced branches and that
    61	 * `.worktrees/` is gitignored. Idempotent: an existing seat is left alone.
    62	 * @param {string} root @param {any} io
    63	 * @returns {Promise<{ok: boolean, worktrees: Array<{seat: string, path: string, branch: string}>, gitignoreChanged: boolean}>}

    65	export async function setupWorktrees(root, io) {
    66	  const p = worktreePaths(root);

    69	  const existing = io.fs.existsSync(gitignorePath) ? io.fs.readFileSync(gitignorePath, 'utf8') : '';

    71	  if (ensured.changed) io.fs.writeFileSync(gitignorePath, ensured.text);
    72	
    73	  const listed = parseWorktreeList((await git(io, root, ['worktree', 'list', '--porcelain'])).stdout);
    74	  /** @type {Array<{seat: string, path: string, branch: string}>} */
    75	  const worktrees = [];
    76	  for (const seat of SEATS) {
    77	    const path = p.seats[seat];
    78	    const present = listed.find((e) => e.path === path);
    79	    if (present) {
    80	      worktrees.push({ seat, path, branch: present.branch ?? seatBranch(seat) });
    81	      continue;
    82	    }
    83	    const branch = seatBranch(seat);
    84	    await git(io, root, ['worktree', 'add', '-b', branch, path]);
    85	    worktrees.push({ seat, path, branch });
    86	  }
    87	  return { ok: true, worktrees, gitignoreChanged: ensured.changed };
    88	}
    89	
    90	/**
    91	 * Preflight before a child runs in a seat: the worktree is listed, its index
    92	 * is clean, and HEAD sits on the expected branch. Refusals name the failed
    93	 * check and never issue destructive git.
    94	 * @param {string} root @param {{seat: string, branch: string}} target @param {any} io
    95	 * @returns {Promise<{ok: true} | {ok: false, check: string, refusal: string}>}
    96	 */
    97	export async function preflightWorktree(root, target, io) {
    98	  const path = worktreePaths(root).seats[target.seat];
    99	  const listed = parseWorktreeList((await git(io, root, ['worktree', 'list', '--porcelain'])).stdout);
   100	  if (!listed.some((e) => e.path === path)) {
   101	    return { ok: false, check: 'missing-worktree', refusal: `worktree ${path} is not listed by git — run setup or self-heal first` };
   102	  }
   103	  const status = (await git(io, path, ['status', '--porcelain'])).stdout.trim();
   104	  if (status.length > 0) {
   105	    return { ok: false, check: 'dirty-index', refusal: `worktree ${path} has a dirty index/working tree — a clean seat is required before a child runs` };
   106	  }
   107	  const head = (await git(io, path, ['rev-parse', '--abbrev-ref', 'HEAD'])).stdout.trim();
   108	  if (head !== target.branch) {
   109	    return { ok: false, check: 'wrong-branch', refusal: `worktree ${path} HEAD is on '${head}', expected branch '${target.branch}'` };
   110	  }
   111	  return { ok: true };
   112	}
   113	
   114	/**
   115	 * Postflight after a child: HEAD still on the expected branch (checked in the
   116	 * seat cwd) and main unmoved from the pre-child capture.
   117	 * @param {string} root @param {{seat: string, branch: string, mainSha: string}} target @param {any} io
   118	 * @returns {Promise<{ok: true} | {ok: false, check: string, refusal: string}>}
   119	 */
   120	export async function postflightWorktree(root, target, io) {
   121	  const path = worktreePaths(root).seats[target.seat];
   122	  const head = (await git(io, path, ['rev-parse', '--abbrev-ref', 'HEAD'])).stdout.trim();
   123	  if (head !== target.branch) {
   124	    return { ok: false, check: 'head-drift', refusal: `the child left HEAD on '${head}' instead of the expected branch '${target.branch}'` };
   125	  }
   126	  const main = (await git(io, root, ['rev-parse', 'main'])).stdout.trim();
   127	  if (main !== target.mainSha) {
   128	    return { ok: false, check: 'main-moved', refusal: `main moved under the child (was ${target.mainSha}, now ${main}) — only the merger writes main` };
   129	  }
   130	  return { ok: true };
   131	}
   132	
   133	/**
   134	 * Full-range attribution scan over main..branch — the merge gate. Every
   135	 * commit must be authored AND committed by the sole author, with no forbidden
   136	 * AI-attribution trailer in the body. The range is never windowed.
   137	 * @param {string} root @param {string} branch @param {any} io
   138	 * @returns {Promise<{ok: true} | {ok: false, offending: string, reason: string}>}
   139	 */
   140	export async function attributionScan(root, branch, io) {
   141	  const out = (await git(io, root, ['log', `main..${branch}`, `--format=${LOG_FORMAT}`])).stdout;
   142	  for (const record of String(out).split('\x1e')) {
   143	    if (record.trim().length === 0) continue;
   144	    const [sha, an, ae, cn, ce, body = ''] = record.replace(/^\n/, '').split('\x00');
   145	    if (an !== SOLE_AUTHOR.name || ae !== SOLE_AUTHOR.email) {
   146	      return { ok: false, offending: sha, reason: `commit ${sha} has a foreign author identity (${an} <${ae}>) — sole-author is required` };
   147	    }
   148	    if (cn !== SOLE_AUTHOR.name || ce !== SOLE_AUTHOR.email) {
   149	      return { ok: false, offending: sha, reason: `commit ${sha} has a foreign committer identity (${cn} <${ce}>) — sole-author is required` };
   150	    }
   151	    if (TRAILER_PATTERNS.some((re) => re.test(body))) {
   152	      return { ok: false, offending: sha, reason: `commit ${sha} carries a forbidden AI-attribution trailer (co-authored/generated-with/emoji) in its message` };
   153	    }
   154	  }
   155	  return { ok: true };
   156	}
   157	
   158	const mergeLockPath = (/** @type {string} */ root) => `${root}/.handoff/loop/merge.lock`;
   159	
   160	/**
   161	 * Merge an approved subtask branch into main — one guarded transaction:
   162	 * acquire the merge lock (a held lock refuses), gate on the attribution scan,
   163	 * merge at the repo root, abort-and-park on conflict, then ff-only sync both
   164	 * seats. The lock is released on EVERY exit path after acquisition.
   165	 * @param {string} root @param {{branch: string}} input @param {any} io
   166	 * @returns {Promise<{ok: true, branch: string} | {ok: false, reason: string}>}
   167	 */
   168	export async function mergeSubtask(root, input, io) {
   169	  const lock = mergeLockPath(root);
   170	  if (io.fs.existsSync(lock)) {
   171	    return { ok: false, reason: `the merge lock at ${lock} is held by another merger — refusing a concurrent merge` };
   172	  }
   173	  ensureDir(io.fs, `${root}/.handoff/loop`);
   174	  atomicWriteJson(io.fs, lock, { host: io.host, pid: io.pid, at: io.now() });
   175	  try {
   176	    const scan = await attributionScan(root, input.branch, io);
   177	    if (scan.ok !== true) return { ok: false, reason: scan.reason };
   178	
   179	    try {
   180	      await git(io, root, ['merge', input.branch]);
   181	    } catch (err) {
   182	      // Conflict (or any merge failure): abort, never auto-resolve — park.
   183	      try {
   184	        await git(io, root, ['merge', '--abort']);
   185	      } catch {
   186	        // Nothing to abort — the failure predates a merge state.
   187	      }
   188	      const msg = /** @type {any} */ (err)?.stdout || /** @type {any} */ (err)?.message || String(err);
   189	      return { ok: false, reason: `merge conflict — aborted and parked for the operator: ${String(msg).split('\n')[0]}` };
   190	    }
   191	
   192	    const seats = worktreePaths(root).seats;
   193	    for (const seat of SEATS) {
   194	      try {
   195	        await git(io, seats[seat], ['merge', '--ff-only', 'main']);
   196	      } catch (err) {
   197	        const msg = /** @type {any} */ (err)?.stderr || /** @type {any} */ (err)?.message || String(err);
   198	        return { ok: false, reason: `worktree wt-${seat} could not fast-forward to main — corruption signal, parked: ${String(msg).split('\n')[0]}` };
   199	      }
   200	    }
   201	    return { ok: true, branch: input.branch };
   202	  } finally {
   203	    try {
   204	      if (io.fs.existsSync(lock)) io.fs.unlinkSync(lock);
   205	    } catch {
   206	      // Best effort — a leaked lock is visible and operator-recoverable.
   207	    }
   208	  }
   209	}
   210	
   211	/**
   212	 * Self-heal a raw-deleted seat: git still lists it but the directory is gone
   213	 * — prune the stale metadata and re-add on the same branch.
   214	 * @param {string} root @param {{seat: string, branch: string}} target @param {any} io
   215	 * @returns {Promise<{ok: boolean, healed: boolean}>}
   216	 */
   217	export async function selfHealWorktree(root, target, io) {
   218	  const path = worktreePaths(root).seats[target.seat];
   219	  const listed = parseWorktreeList((await git(io, root, ['worktree', 'list', '--porcelain'])).stdout);
   220	  const stale = listed.some((e) => e.path === path) && !io.fs.existsSync(path);
   221	  if (!stale) return { ok: true, healed: false };
   222	  await git(io, root, ['worktree', 'prune']);
   223	  await git(io, root, ['worktree', 'add', path, target.branch]);
   224	  return { ok: true, healed: true };
   225	}
   226	
   227	/**
   228	 * Tear down BOTH seats: remove the worktrees and delete their namespaced
   229	 * branches. Anything outside .worktrees/wt-* or baton/wt-* is never touched,
   230	 * so a teardown leaves the repo a plain git repo (acceptance constraint 5).
   231	 * @param {string} root @param {any} io
   232	 * @returns {Promise<{ok: boolean, removed: string[]}>}
   233	 */
   234	export async function teardownWorktrees(root, io) {
   235	  const p = worktreePaths(root);
   236	  const listed = parseWorktreeList((await git(io, root, ['worktree', 'list', '--porcelain'])).stdout);
   237	  /** @type {string[]} */
   238	  const removed = [];
   239	  for (const seat of SEATS) {
   240	    const entry = listed.find((e) => e.path === p.seats[seat]);
   241	    if (!entry) continue;
   242	    await git(io, root, ['worktree', 'remove', '--force', p.seats[seat]]);
   243	    // Delete the seat's CURRENT branch and its setup base branch — a seat
   244	    // that moved on to subtask branches orphans its base otherwise
   245	    // (acceptance constraint 5: teardown leaves a plain git repo). Only
   246	    // namespaced branches are ever deleted.
   247	    /** @type {Set<string>} */
   248	    const toDelete = new Set();
   249	    if (entry.branch && WORKTREE_BRANCH_RE.test(entry.branch)) toDelete.add(entry.branch);
   250	    toDelete.add(seatBranch(seat));
   251	    for (const branch of toDelete) {
   252	      if (!WORKTREE_BRANCH_RE.test(branch)) continue;
   253	      try {
   254	        await git(io, root, ['branch', '-D', branch]);
   255	      } catch {
   256	        // Already gone (current === base, or deleted earlier) — fine.
   257	      }
   258	    }
   259	    removed.push(seat);
   260	  }
   261	  return { ok: true, removed };
   262	}
   263	
   264	/**
   265	 * Branch jail: only baton/wt-* branches may ever be deleted through this
   266	 * layer. An out-of-namespace ref refuses without touching git.
   267	 * @param {string} root @param {string} branch @param {any} io
   268	 * @returns {Promise<{ok: true} | {ok: false, error: string}>}
   269	 */
   270	export async function deleteBranch(root, branch, io) {
   271	  if (!WORKTREE_BRANCH_RE.test(branch)) {
   272	    return { ok: false, error: `refused: '${branch}' is outside the baton/wt- namespace — the loop never deletes non-loop branches` };
   273	  }
   274	  await git(io, root, ['branch', '-D', branch]);
   275	  return { ok: true };
   276	}
