    29	// Flags that consume a value — needed to split positionals from flag tokens
    30	// without mistaking a flag's value for a positional. Keep in sync with the
    31	// strict spec below plus the implied common string flag (--root).
    32	const STRING_FLAGS = new Set(['root', 'approve-smoke']);
    33	
    34	/**
    35	 * `baton loop <subcommand>` — the Layer-2 goal-loop surface. v1 subcommands:
    36	 * `init "<goal>"` (scaffold loop.json; two-phase, idempotent, dry-run-able).
    37	 * Supervisor-side by design: the BATON_SUPERVISED_CHILD guard deliberately
    38	 * does NOT apply here.
    39	 * @param {string[]} args @param {any} io
    40	 * @returns {Promise<number>}
    41	 */
    42	export async function cmdLoop(args, io) {
    43	  /** @type {string[]} */
    44	  const positionals = [];
    45	  /** @type {string[]} */
    46	  const flagTokens = [];
    47	  for (let i = 0; i < args.length; i += 1) {
    48	    const a = args[i];
    49	    if (typeof a === 'string' && a.startsWith('--')) {
    50	      flagTokens.push(a);
    51	      if (STRING_FLAGS.has(a.slice(2)) && i + 1 < args.length) {
    52	        i += 1;
    53	        flagTokens.push(args[i]);
    54	      }
    55	    } else {
    56	      positionals.push(a);
    57	    }
    58	  }
    59	
    60	  const parsed = parseFlagsStrict(flagTokens, { 'dry-run': 'boolean', 'approve-smoke': 'string' });
    61	  if (parsed.error !== undefined) return usageError(io, parsed.flags, 'loop', parsed.error);
    62	  const flags = parsed.flags;
    63	
    64	  const sub = positionals[0];
    65	  if (sub === undefined) return usageError(io, flags, 'loop', 'a subcommand is required — try: baton loop init "<goal>" or baton loop run');
    66	  if (sub === 'run') {
    67	    if (positionals.length > 1) return usageError(io, flags, 'loop', `unexpected argument '${positionals[1]}'`);
    68	    if (flags['approve-smoke'] === '') return usageError(io, flags, 'loop', '--approve-smoke requires a value (the token from smoke-approval.json)');
    69	    return runLoop(flags, io);
    70	  }
    71	  if (sub !== 'init') return usageError(io, flags, 'loop', `unknown subcommand '${sub}' (supported: init, run)`);
    72	
    73	  const goal = positionals[1];
    74	  if (goal === undefined) return usageError(io, flags, 'loop', 'loop init requires a goal — baton loop init "<goal>"');
    75	  if (positionals.length > 2) return usageError(io, flags, 'loop', `unexpected argument '${positionals[2]}'`);
    76	
    77	  const root = resolveRoot(io, flags);
    78	  const path = `${root}/loop.json`;
    79	
    80	  const finish = (/** @type {any} */ data, /** @type {string} */ human) => {
    81	    if (flags.json) emitEnvelope(io, { ok: true, data });
    82	    else io.stdout.write(`${human}\n`);
    83	    return 0;
    84	  };
    85	
    86	  // Idempotent: an existing spec is the user's — never overwritten.
    87	  if (io.fs.existsSync(path)) {
    88	    return finish({ created: false, path }, `baton loop init: ${path} already exists — left untouched`);
    89	  }
    90	  if (flags['dry-run'] === true) {
    91	    return finish(
    92	      { created: false, path, dryRun: true },
    93	      `baton loop init (dry-run): would write ${path} — the default baton/loop@1 spec with goal "${goal}"`,
    94	    );
    95	  }
    96	  ensureDir(io.fs, root);
    97	  atomicWriteJson(io.fs, path, defaultLoopSpec(goal));
    98	  return finish({ created: true, path }, `baton loop init: wrote ${path} (goal "${goal}") — edit constraints/smoke, then run baton loop run`);
    99	}
   100	
   101	// Exit codes for `loop run` (frozen by the loop-run test contract):
   102	// 0 done / awaiting-smoke-approval · 1 live-lock refusal · 2 usage error ·
   103	// 3 escalated · 4 parked.
   104	const EXIT_ESCALATED = 3;
   105	const EXIT_PARKED = 4;
   106	
   107	/** @param {any} g */
   108	const gitHeadOf = (g) => g?.headSha ?? 'none';
   109	/** @param {any} g */
   110	const gitContentOf = (g) => g?.contentDigest ?? dedupeKey(g ?? null);
   111	
   112	/**
   113	 * The supervisor state machine: drives the spec's phases through headless
   114	 * role children, records every transition in the journal-replayable loop
   115	 * state, enforces the 5-cap, pauses at the smoke gate, and hands limit
   116	 * deaths to the failover transaction. Children run via the injectable seam
   117	 * `io.superviseChild ?? superviseChild`.
   118	 * @param {Record<string, string | boolean>} flags @param {any} io
   119	 * @returns {Promise<number>}
   120	 */
   121	async function runLoop(flags, io) {
   122	  const root = resolveRoot(io, flags);
   123	  const specPath = `${root}/loop.json`;
   124	  const p = loopPaths(root);
   125	  const lockPath = `${p.dir}/supervisor.lock`;
   126	
   127	  // Preconditions — before any lock or spawn.
   128	  const rawSpec = safeReadJson(io.fs, specPath);
   129	  if (!rawSpec.ok) {
   130	    return usageError(io, flags, 'loop', `no loop.json at ${specPath} — scaffold one with: baton loop init "<goal>"`);
   131	  }
   132	  const { config } = loadConfig(root, io);
   133	  if (!config) return usageError(io, flags, 'loop', `no readable baton.config.json at ${root} — the role matrix is required`);
   134	  const validated = validateLoopSpec(rawSpec.value, config);
   135	  if (validated.ok !== true) {
   136	    return usageError(io, flags, 'loop', `loop.json is invalid:\n  - ${validated.errors.join('\n  - ')}`);
   137	  }
   138	  const spec = validated.spec;
   139	
   140	  // Supervisor run lock — same provably-dead discipline as the bundle lock:
   141	  // a live same-host owner is refused; a dead one is reclaimed.
   142	  const existingLock = safeReadJson(io.fs, lockPath);
   143	  if (existingLock.ok && existingLock.value && typeof existingLock.value.pid === 'number') {
   144	    const other = existingLock.value;
   145	    const alive = typeof io.processAlive === 'function' ? io.processAlive(other.pid) : true;
   146	    const sameProcess = other.pid === io.pid && other.startTime === io.startTime;
   147	    if (!sameProcess && (other.host !== io.host || alive)) {
   148	      io.stderr.write(`baton loop run: another supervisor (pid ${other.pid} on ${other.host}, run ${other.runId}) holds the run lock — refusing a second supervisor\n`);
   149	      return 1;
   150	    }
   151	    io.stderr.write(`baton loop run: reclaimed the run lock from a provably-dead supervisor (pid ${other.pid})\n`);
   152	  }
   153	
   154	  // Load or initialize the run state.
   155	  let { state } = await loadLoopState(root, io);
   156	  if (state === null) {
   157	    state = initLoopState(spec, io);
   158	    await writeLoopState(root, state, io);
   159	  }
   160	  const runId = state.runId;
   161	  ensureDir(io.fs, p.dir);
   162	  atomicWriteJson(io.fs, lockPath, { host: io.host, pid: io.pid, startTime: io.startTime, runId });
   163	
   164	  /** Apply one event, journal it, persist the snapshot — one transition. */
   165	  const transition = async (/** @type {any} */ ev) => {
   166	    const next = applyLoopEvent(state, ev);
   167	    const seq = await appendLoopEvent(root, ev, io);
   168	    state = { ...next, journalSeq: seq };
   169	    await writeLoopState(root, state, io);
   170	    return state;
   171	  };
   172	
   173	  const table = loadSignatures({ builtinPath: BUILTIN_SIGNATURES }, io);
   174	  const runner = io.superviseChild ?? superviseChild;
   175	  const sessionHint = `loop-${runId}`;
   176	  /** @type {string[]} */
   177	  let avoid = [];
   178	  /** @type {Array<{platform: string, model: string}>} */
   179	  let avoidEntries = [];
   180	  let childSeq = 0;
   181	
   182	  try {
   183	    // --approve-smoke: verify the presented token against RECOMPUTED inputs;
   184	    // drift parks (the human approved a state that no longer exists).
   185	    if (typeof flags['approve-smoke'] === 'string') {
   186	      if (state.status !== LOOP_STATUS.AWAITING_SMOKE_APPROVAL) {
   187	        return usageError(io, flags, 'loop', `--approve-smoke only applies while the run awaits smoke approval (status: ${state.status})`);
   188	      }
   189	      const record = safeReadJson(io.fs, `${p.dir}/smoke-approval.json`);
   190	      if (!record.ok) return usageError(io, flags, 'loop', 'no smoke-approval.json found — run the loop to the smoke gate first');
   191	      const git = await gitSnapshot({ execFile: io.execFile, cwd: root, fs: io.fs });
   192	      const currentInputs = {
   193	        stateDigest: dedupeKey(io.fs.readFileSync(p.state, 'utf8')),
   194	        smokeCmd: String(spec.smoke?.cmd ?? ''),
   195	        smokeOutputDigest: String(record.value?.inputs?.smokeOutputDigest ?? ''),
   196	        gitHead: gitHeadOf(git),
   197	        gitContentDigest: gitContentOf(git),
   198	        childAssignment: String(record.value?.inputs?.childAssignment ?? ''),
   199	      };
   200	      const verify = verifySmokeToken(String(flags['approve-smoke']), currentInputs);
   201	      if (verify.ok !== true) {
   202	        const drifted = 'driftedInputs' in verify ? verify.driftedInputs.join(', ') : 'unknown';
   203	        await transition({ type: LOOP_EVENT.PARK, reason: `smoke approval token is stale — drifted inputs: ${drifted}; re-run the smoke gate` });
   204	        io.stderr.write(`baton loop run: smoke approval REFUSED — inputs drifted since the token was issued (${drifted}); the run is parked for a fresh smoke pass\n`);
   205	        return EXIT_PARKED;
   206	      }
   207	      await transition({ type: LOOP_EVENT.SMOKE_APPROVE, token: flags['approve-smoke'], verified: true });
   208	      io.stdout.write('baton loop run: smoke approval verified — continuing the run\n');
   209	    }
   210	
   211	    if (state.status === LOOP_STATUS.ESCALATED) {
   212	      io.stderr.write(`baton loop run: the run is escalated (gate ${state.escalation?.gate}) — see ${p.dir}/ESCALATION.md\n`);
   213	      return EXIT_ESCALATED;
   214	    }
   215	    if (state.status === LOOP_STATUS.PARKED) {
   216	      io.stderr.write(`baton loop run: the run is parked — ${state.parkReason ?? 'no reason recorded'}\n`);
   217	      return EXIT_PARKED;
   218	    }
   219	    if (state.status === LOOP_STATUS.AWAITING_SMOKE_APPROVAL) {
   220	      io.stdout.write(`baton loop run: awaiting smoke approval — resume with: baton loop run --approve-smoke <token from ${p.dir}/smoke-approval.json>\n`);
   221	      return 0;
   222	    }
   223	
   224	    // ---- The phase loop. ---------------------------------------------------
   225	    while (state.status === LOOP_STATUS.RUNNING && state.phaseIndex < state.phaseCount) {
   226	      const phase = spec.phases[state.phaseIndex];
   227	      const cap = spec.budgets.iterationCap;
   228	      let findings = '';
   229	      let failoverAttempt = 0;
   230	      /** @type {any | null} */
   231	      let forcedAssignment = null;
   232	      /** @type {string | null} */
   233	      let forcedPrompt = null;
   234	      let phaseDone = false;
   235	
   236	      while (!phaseDone) {
   237	        // The cap gates the SPAWN: at the cap the reducer escalates and a
   238	        // 6th child never starts.
   239	        if ((state.iterations?.[phase.id] ?? 0) >= cap) {
   240	          await transition({ type: LOOP_EVENT.GATE_ITERATION, gate: phase.id, verdict: 'BLOCKED' });
   241	          atomicWriteText(
   242	            io.fs,
   243	            `${p.dir}/ESCALATION.md`,
   244	            `# Loop escalation\n\nGate '${phase.id}' exhausted its ${cap}-iteration cap on run ${runId}.\nLast findings:\n\n${findings}\n\nResolve the findings, then resume with baton loop run.\n`,
   245	          );
   246	          io.stderr.write(`baton loop run: gate '${phase.id}' hit the ${cap}-iteration cap — escalated (see ${p.dir}/ESCALATION.md)\n`);
   247	          return EXIT_ESCALATED;
   248	        }
   249	        if (childSeq >= spec.budgets.maxChildrenPerPhase * spec.phases.length) {
   250	          await transition({ type: LOOP_EVENT.PARK, reason: 'total child budget exhausted' });
   251	          return EXIT_PARKED;
   252	        }
   253	
   254	        const resolved =
   255	          forcedAssignment ??
   256	          (() => {
   257	            const r = resolveRoles({ config, to: 'claude-code', avoid, avoidEntries, probes: null }).assignments[phase.role];
   258	            return r && r.mode !== 'unavailable' ? r : null;
   259	          })();
   260	        if (!resolved) {
   261	          await transition({ type: LOOP_EVENT.PARK, reason: `no eligible platform/model for role '${phase.role}'` });
   262	          io.stderr.write(`baton loop run: no eligible assignment for role '${phase.role}' — parked\n`);
   263	          return EXIT_PARKED;
   264	        }
   265	        const assignment = { ...resolved, role: phase.role };
   266	        const prompt =
   267	          forcedPrompt ??
   268	          [
   269	            `You are the ${phase.role} for loop run ${runId}, phase '${phase.id}'.`,
   270	            `Goal: ${spec.goal}`,
   271	            spec.constraints.length > 0 ? `Constraints:\n${spec.constraints.map((/** @type {string} */ c) => `- ${c}`).join('\n')}` : '',
   272	            findings ? `The previous attempt was BLOCKED with these findings — address every one:\n${findings}` : '',
   273	            "End with exactly:\nVERDICT: APPROVED | APPROVED_WITH_NOTES | BLOCKED\nFINDINGS: numbered findings, or 'none'",
   274	          ]
   275	            .filter(Boolean)
   276	            .join('\n\n');
   277	        forcedAssignment = null;
   278	        forcedPrompt = null;
   279	
   280	        childSeq += 1;
   281	        const logPath = `${p.dir}/children/${String(childSeq).padStart(3, '0')}-${phase.id}.log`;
   282	        ensureDir(io.fs, `${p.dir}/children`);
   283	        const childSpec = buildChildArgv(assignment, prompt, { root });
   284	        const result = await runner(childSpec, {
   285	          timeoutMs: spec.budgets.perRoleTimeoutMin * 60_000,
   286	          graceMs: 10_000,
   287	          logPath,
   288	          maxLogBytes: 1_000_000,
   289	          platform: assignment.platform,
   290	        });
   291	
   292	        // Classification reads the child's LOG (the frozen transcript), so a
   293	        // limit banner routes to failover even when a verdict parsed.
   294	        const transcript = io.fs.existsSync(logPath) ? io.fs.readFileSync(logPath, 'utf8') : '';
   295	        const cls = classify({ text: transcript, exitCode: result.exitCode ?? 0, platform: assignment.platform, table }).class;
   296	
   297	        if (cls === 'usage-limit' || cls === 'model-unavailable' || cls === 'other-error' || cls === 'throttle' || cls === 'auth') {
   298	          failoverAttempt += 1;
   299	          const decision = await runFailover({
   300	            root,
   301	            io,
   302	            config,
   303	            role: phase.role,
   304	            assignment,
   305	            transcript,
   306	            exitCode: result.exitCode ?? 1,
   307	            sessionHint,
   308	            probes: null,
   309	            avoid,
   310	            avoidEntries,
   311	            attempt: failoverAttempt,
   312	            loopState: { runId, phaseIndex: state.phaseIndex, iterations: state.iterations, status: state.status },
   313	          });
   314	          if (decision.action === 'park') {
   315	            await transition({ type: LOOP_EVENT.PARK, reason: decision.reason });
   316	            io.stderr.write(`baton loop run: parked — ${decision.reason}${'resumeAt' in decision && decision.resumeAt ? ` (resume around ${decision.resumeAt})` : ''}\n`);
   317	            return EXIT_PARKED;
   318	          }
   319	          if (decision.action === 'relaunch') {
   320	            // Platform avoidance is PER-DEATH, not cumulative: runFailover
   321	            // already avoided the just-died platform when it resolved the
   322	            // relaunch. Accumulating here would exhaust every platform on a
   323	            // chained A→B→A failover and park instead of returning to A
   324	            // (acceptance constraint 2). Entry-level avoidance (dead MODELS)
   325	            // does accumulate — a rejected model stays rejected.
   326	            if (Array.isArray(decision.avoidEntries)) avoidEntries = decision.avoidEntries;
   327	            forcedAssignment = { ...decision.assignment, role: phase.role };
   328	            forcedPrompt = typeof decision.prompt === 'string' && decision.prompt.length > 0 ? decision.prompt : null;
   329	            continue; // relaunch the same phase on the new assignment
   330	          }
   331	          continue; // retry: re-spawn the same phase once more
   332	        }
   333	
   334	        const verdict = String(result.verdict ?? 'BLOCKED');
   335	        if (verdict === 'APPROVED' || verdict === 'APPROVED_WITH_NOTES') {
   336	          await transition({ type: LOOP_EVENT.PHASE_ADVANCE });
   337	          phaseDone = true;
   338	
   339	          // The smoke gate fires right after the smoke-build phase completes.
   340	          if (phase.id === 'smoke-build' && typeof spec.smoke?.cmd === 'string' && spec.smoke.cmd.length > 0) {
   341	            const [cmd, ...cmdArgs] = spec.smoke.cmd.split(/\s+/);
   342	            let smokeOut = '';
   343	            try {
   344	              const r = await io.execFile(cmd, cmdArgs, { cwd: root });
   345	              smokeOut = `${r?.stdout ?? ''}${r?.stderr ?? ''}`;
   346	            } catch (err) {
   347	              smokeOut = `smoke command failed: ${/** @type {any} */ (err)?.message ?? String(err)}`;
   348	            }
   349	            await transition({ type: LOOP_EVENT.SMOKE_AWAIT });
   350	            const git = await gitSnapshot({ execFile: io.execFile, cwd: root, fs: io.fs });
   351	            const inputs = {
   352	              stateDigest: dedupeKey(io.fs.readFileSync(p.state, 'utf8')),
   353	              smokeCmd: spec.smoke.cmd,
   354	              smokeOutputDigest: dedupeKey(smokeOut),
   355	              gitHead: gitHeadOf(git),
   356	              gitContentDigest: gitContentOf(git),
   357	              childAssignment: JSON.stringify({ platform: assignment.platform, model: assignment.model }),
   358	            };
   359	            const token = smokeApprovalToken(inputs);
   360	            atomicWriteJson(io.fs, `${p.dir}/smoke-approval.json`, { token, inputs, issuedAt: io.now() });
   361	            atomicWriteText(
   362	              io.fs,
   363	              `${p.dir}/SMOKE-REVIEW.md`,
   364	              `# Smoke gate — human approval required\n\nRun: ${runId}\nCommand: \`${spec.smoke.cmd}\`\nExpected: ${spec.smoke.expect ?? '(unspecified)'}\n\n## Output\n\n\`\`\`\n${smokeOut}\n\`\`\`\n\nApprove with:\n\n    baton loop run --approve-smoke ${token}\n\nThe token is bound to the current state/output/git — any drift refuses it.\n`,
   365	            );
   366	            io.stdout.write(`baton loop run: smoke gate reached — review ${p.dir}/SMOKE-REVIEW.md and approve with --approve-smoke <token>\n`);
   367	            return 0;
   368	          }
   369	        } else {
   370	          // BLOCKED (or unparseable, already coerced to BLOCKED upstream).
   371	          findings = String(result.findings ?? '');
   372	          const after = await transition({ type: LOOP_EVENT.GATE_ITERATION, gate: phase.id, verdict });
   373	          if (after.status === LOOP_STATUS.ESCALATED) {
   374	            atomicWriteText(
   375	              io.fs,
   376	              `${p.dir}/ESCALATION.md`,
   377	              `# Loop escalation\n\nGate '${phase.id}' exhausted its ${cap}-iteration cap on run ${runId}.\nLast findings:\n\n${findings}\n`,
   378	            );
   379	            io.stderr.write(`baton loop run: gate '${phase.id}' escalated at the cap — see ${p.dir}/ESCALATION.md\n`);
   380	            return EXIT_ESCALATED;
   381	          }
   382	        }
   383	      }
   384	    }
   385	
   386	    if (state.status === LOOP_STATUS.DONE || state.phaseIndex >= state.phaseCount) {
   387	      io.stdout.write(`baton loop run: done — every phase completed (run ${runId})\n`);
   388	      return 0;
   389	    }
   390	    io.stdout.write(`baton loop run: stopped in status ${state.status}\n`);
   391	    return state.status === LOOP_STATUS.PARKED ? EXIT_PARKED : state.status === LOOP_STATUS.ESCALATED ? EXIT_ESCALATED : 0;
   392	  } finally {
   393	    // The lock is per-invocation: release on every exit path so a paused run
   394	    // (awaiting approval) can be resumed by the next invocation.
   395	    try {
   396	      if (io.fs.existsSync(lockPath)) io.fs.unlinkSync(lockPath);
   397	    } catch {
   398	      // Best effort — a stale lock is recoverable via the provably-dead path.
   399	    }
   400	  }
   401	}

 succeeded in 0ms:
     1	/**
     2	 * `baton pipeline run` — the Layer-3 dual-worktree preset over the loop
     3	 * engine. Subtasks alternate writer seats (index 0 → wt-a/worker-a, 1 →
     4	 * wt-b/worker-b, …); the reviewer runs READ-ONLY from the OTHER seat with
     5	 * that seat's worker model under the subtask-reviewer role; the merger child
     6	 * (read-only) adversarially re-checks, and on its APPROVED the SUPERVISOR
     7	 * merges through worktrees.mergeSubtask (attribution-gated, locked, ff-only
     8	 * seat syncs). Plan: docs/plans/2026-07-18-goal-loop-worktree-pipeline.md
     9	 * §"baton pipeline (Layer 3 — a loop preset)".
    10	 */
    11	import {
    12	  loopPaths,
    13	  initLoopState,
    14	  applyLoopEvent,
    15	  writeLoopState,
    16	  appendLoopEvent,
    17	  LOOP_EVENT,
    18	  LOOP_STATUS,
    19	} from '../loop/state.mjs';
    20	import { buildChildArgv, superviseChild } from '../loop/children.mjs';
    21	import { setupWorktrees, preflightWorktree, postflightWorktree, mergeSubtask, worktreePaths } from '../loop/worktrees.mjs';
    22	import { resolveRoles } from '../roles/resolve.mjs';
    23	import { loadConfig } from '../roles/matrix.mjs';
    24	import { atomicWriteText, ensureDir, safeReadJson } from '../util/fsx.mjs';
    25	import { emitEnvelope, usageError, parseFlagsStrict, resolveRoot } from './shared.mjs';
    26	
    27	const STRING_FLAGS = new Set(['root']);
    28	const EXIT_ESCALATED = 3;
    29	const EXIT_PARKED = 4;
    30	
    31	/**
    32	 * @param {string[]} args @param {any} io
    33	 * @returns {Promise<number>}
    34	 */
    35	export async function cmdPipeline(args, io) {
    36	  /** @type {string[]} */
    37	  const positionals = [];
    38	  /** @type {string[]} */
    39	  const flagTokens = [];
    40	  for (let i = 0; i < args.length; i += 1) {
    41	    const a = args[i];
    42	    if (typeof a === 'string' && a.startsWith('--')) {
    43	      flagTokens.push(a);
    44	      if (STRING_FLAGS.has(a.slice(2)) && i + 1 < args.length) {
    45	        i += 1;
    46	        flagTokens.push(args[i]);
    47	      }
    48	    } else {
    49	      positionals.push(a);
    50	    }
    51	  }
    52	  const parsed = parseFlagsStrict(flagTokens, {});
    53	  if (parsed.error !== undefined) return usageError(io, parsed.flags, 'pipeline', parsed.error);
    54	  const flags = parsed.flags;
    55	
    56	  const sub = positionals[0];
    57	  if (sub === undefined) return usageError(io, flags, 'pipeline', 'a subcommand is required — try: baton pipeline run');
    58	  if (sub !== 'run') return usageError(io, flags, 'pipeline', `unknown subcommand '${sub}' (supported: run)`);
    59	  if (positionals.length > 1) return usageError(io, flags, 'pipeline', `unexpected argument '${positionals[1]}'`);
    60	
    61	  return runPipeline(flags, io);
    62	}
    63	
    64	/** Validate the spec's subtasks — every rejection names its offender.
    65	 * @param {any} subtasks @returns {string | null} */
    66	function validateSubtasks(subtasks) {
    67	  if (!Array.isArray(subtasks) || subtasks.length === 0) {
    68	    return 'loop.json needs a non-empty `subtasks` array ([{id, title}]) for a pipeline run';
    69	  }
    70	  const seen = new Set();
    71	  for (let i = 0; i < subtasks.length; i += 1) {
    72	    const st = subtasks[i];
    73	    if (typeof st?.id !== 'string' || st.id.length === 0) return `subtasks[${i}] is missing a string id`;
    74	    if (typeof st?.title !== 'string' || st.title.length === 0) return `subtasks[${i}] ('${st.id}') is missing a string title`;
    75	    if (seen.has(st.id)) return `subtasks carry a duplicate id '${st.id}' — ids name branches and must be unique`;
    76	    seen.add(st.id);
    77	  }
    78	  return null;
    79	}
    80	
    81	/**
    82	 * @param {Record<string, string | boolean>} flags @param {any} io
    83	 * @returns {Promise<number>}
    84	 */
    85	async function runPipeline(flags, io) {
    86	  const root = resolveRoot(io, flags);
    87	  const p = loopPaths(root);
    88	
    89	  const rawSpec = safeReadJson(io.fs, `${root}/loop.json`);
    90	  if (!rawSpec.ok) return usageError(io, flags, 'pipeline', `no loop.json at ${root} — scaffold one with: baton loop init "<goal>"`);
    91	  const spec = rawSpec.value;
    92	  const subtaskError = validateSubtasks(spec?.subtasks);
    93	  if (subtaskError !== null) return usageError(io, flags, 'pipeline', subtaskError);
    94	  const { config } = loadConfig(root, io);
    95	  if (!config) return usageError(io, flags, 'pipeline', `no readable baton.config.json at ${root} — the role matrix is required`);
    96	  const cap = typeof spec?.budgets?.iterationCap === 'number' ? spec.budgets.iterationCap : 5;
    97	  const timeoutMs = (typeof spec?.budgets?.perRoleTimeoutMin === 'number' ? spec.budgets.perRoleTimeoutMin : 30) * 60_000;
    98	
    99	  /** @param {string} role @returns {any | null} */
   100	  const resolveOne = (role) => {
   101	    const a = resolveRoles({ config, to: 'claude-code', avoid: [], avoidEntries: [], probes: null }).assignments[role];
   102	    return a && a.mode !== 'unavailable' ? a : null;
   103	  };
   104	
   105	  // The synthesized phase list makes the loop state machine (phase count,
   106	  // done detection, journal replay) work unchanged for the subtask cycle.
   107	  const subtasks = spec.subtasks;
   108	  const stateSpec = {
   109	    goal: spec.goal,
   110	    phases: subtasks.map((/** @type {any} */ st, /** @type {number} */ i) => ({ id: `subtask-${st.id}`, role: `worker-${i % 2 === 0 ? 'a' : 'b'}` })),
   111	  };
   112	  /** @type {any} */
   113	  let state = initLoopState(stateSpec, io);
   114	  await writeLoopState(root, state, io);
   115	  const runner = io.superviseChild ?? superviseChild;
   116	  const seats = worktreePaths(root).seats;
   117	  let childSeq = 0;
   118	
   119	  const transition = async (/** @type {any} */ ev) => {
   120	    const next = applyLoopEvent(state, ev);
   121	    const seq = await appendLoopEvent(root, ev, io);
   122	    state = { ...next, journalSeq: seq };
   123	    await writeLoopState(root, state, io);
   124	    return state;
   125	  };
   126	  const park = async (/** @type {string} */ reason) => {
   127	    await transition({ type: LOOP_EVENT.PARK, reason });
   128	    io.stderr.write(`baton pipeline run: parked — ${reason}\n`);
   129	    return EXIT_PARKED;
   130	  };
   131	
   132	  await setupWorktrees(root, io);
   133	
   134	  const spawn = async (/** @type {any} */ assignment, /** @type {string} */ prompt, /** @type {string} */ seatPath, /** @type {string} */ label) => {
   135	    childSeq += 1;
   136	    const logPath = `${p.dir}/children/${String(childSeq).padStart(3, '0')}-${label}.log`;
   137	    ensureDir(io.fs, `${p.dir}/children`);
   138	    const childSpec = buildChildArgv(assignment, prompt, { root: seatPath });
   139	    return runner(childSpec, { timeoutMs, graceMs: 10_000, logPath, maxLogBytes: 1_000_000, platform: assignment.platform });
   140	  };
   141	
   142	  for (let i = 0; i < subtasks.length; i += 1) {
   143	    const st = subtasks[i];
   144	    const seat = i % 2 === 0 ? 'a' : 'b';
   145	    const other = seat === 'a' ? 'b' : 'a';
   146	    const branch = `baton/wt-${seat}/subtask-${st.id}`;
   147	    const gate = `subtask-${st.id}-review`;
   148	
   149	    const writerAssignment = resolveOne(`worker-${seat}`);
   150	    const reviewerModel = resolveOne(`worker-${other}`);
   151	    const mergerModel = resolveOne('merger');
   152	    if (!writerAssignment || !reviewerModel || !mergerModel) {
   153	      return park(`no eligible assignment for subtask '${st.id}' (worker-${seat}/worker-${other}/merger must all resolve)`);
   154	    }
   155	
   156	    // Start the subtask branch in the writer's seat, then verify the seat.
   157	    await io.execFile('git', ['checkout', '-b', branch], { cwd: seats[seat] });
   158	    const pre = await preflightWorktree(root, { seat, branch }, io);
   159	    if (pre.ok !== true) return park(`preflight refused subtask '${st.id}': ${pre.refusal}`);
   160	    const mainSha = (await io.execFile('git', ['rev-parse', 'main'], { cwd: root })).stdout.trim();
   161	
   162	    let findings = '';
   163	    let merged = false;
   164	    while (!merged) {
   165	      if ((state.iterations?.[gate] ?? 0) >= cap) {
   166	        await transition({ type: LOOP_EVENT.GATE_ITERATION, gate, verdict: 'BLOCKED' });
   167	        atomicWriteText(
   168	          io.fs,
   169	          `${p.dir}/ESCALATION.md`,
   170	          `# Pipeline escalation\n\nGate '${gate}' exhausted its ${cap}-iteration cap.\nLast findings:\n\n${findings}\n`,
   171	        );
   172	        io.stderr.write(`baton pipeline run: gate '${gate}' hit the ${cap}-iteration cap — escalated (see ${p.dir}/ESCALATION.md)\n`);
   173	        return EXIT_ESCALATED;
   174	      }
   175	
   176	      // Writer (write-capable, its own seat).
   177	      const writerPrompt = [
   178	        `You are the writer (worker-${seat}) for subtask '${st.id}': ${st.title}.`,
   179	        `Goal: ${spec.goal}. Work ONLY on branch ${branch} in your worktree; commit your work there.`,
   180	        findings ? `The previous attempt was BLOCKED — address every finding:\n${findings}` : '',
   181	      ]
   182	        .filter(Boolean)
   183	        .join('\n\n');
   184	      await spawn({ ...writerAssignment, role: `worker-${seat}` }, writerPrompt, seats[seat], `${st.id}-writer`);
   185	
   186	      const post = await postflightWorktree(root, { seat, branch, mainSha }, io);
   187	      if (post.ok !== true) return park(`postflight refused subtask '${st.id}': ${post.refusal}`);
   188	
   189	      // Reviewer (read-only, the OTHER seat's model and cwd, subtask-reviewer role).
   190	      const reviewPrompt = [
   191	        `You are the subtask-reviewer for subtask '${st.id}': ${st.title}.`,
   192	        `Review the diff of ${branch} against main from the ${other} seat (fresh context).`,
   193	        "End with exactly:\nVERDICT: APPROVED | APPROVED_WITH_NOTES | BLOCKED\nFINDINGS: numbered findings, or 'none'",
   194	      ].join('\n\n');
   195	      const review = await spawn({ ...reviewerModel, role: 'subtask-reviewer' }, reviewPrompt, seats[other], `${st.id}-review`);
   196	      if (review.verdict !== 'APPROVED' && review.verdict !== 'APPROVED_WITH_NOTES') {
   197	        findings = String(review.findings ?? '');
   198	        const after = await transition({ type: LOOP_EVENT.GATE_ITERATION, gate, verdict: 'BLOCKED' });
   199	        if (after.status === LOOP_STATUS.ESCALATED) {
   200	          atomicWriteText(io.fs, `${p.dir}/ESCALATION.md`, `# Pipeline escalation\n\nGate '${gate}' exhausted its ${cap}-iteration cap.\nLast findings:\n\n${findings}\n`);
   201	          return EXIT_ESCALATED;
   202	        }
   203	        continue;
   204	      }
   205	
   206	      // Merger (read-only child — it re-checks; the SUPERVISOR merges).
   207	      const mergerPrompt = [
   208	        `You are the merger for subtask '${st.id}'. Adversarially re-check ${branch} against main before it merges.`,
   209	        "End with exactly:\nVERDICT: APPROVED | APPROVED_WITH_NOTES | BLOCKED\nFINDINGS: numbered findings, or 'none'",
   210	      ].join('\n\n');
   211	      const mergerVerdict = await spawn({ ...mergerModel, role: 'merger' }, mergerPrompt, root, `${st.id}-merge-check`);
   212	      if (mergerVerdict.verdict !== 'APPROVED' && mergerVerdict.verdict !== 'APPROVED_WITH_NOTES') {
   213	        findings = String(mergerVerdict.findings ?? '');
   214	        const after = await transition({ type: LOOP_EVENT.GATE_ITERATION, gate, verdict: 'BLOCKED' });
   215	        if (after.status === LOOP_STATUS.ESCALATED) {
   216	          atomicWriteText(io.fs, `${p.dir}/ESCALATION.md`, `# Pipeline escalation\n\nGate '${gate}' exhausted its ${cap}-iteration cap.\nLast findings:\n\n${findings}\n`);
   217	          return EXIT_ESCALATED;
   218	        }
   219	        continue;
   220	      }
   221	
   222	      const merge = await mergeSubtask(root, { branch }, io);
   223	      if (merge.ok !== true) return park(`merge of '${branch}' refused: ${merge.reason}`);
   224	      merged = true;
   225	    }
   226	
   227	    await transition({ type: LOOP_EVENT.PHASE_ADVANCE });
   228	  }
   229	
   230	  if (flags.json) emitEnvelope(io, { ok: true, data: { status: state.status, subtasks: subtasks.length } });
   231	  else io.stdout.write(`baton pipeline run: done — ${subtasks.length} subtask(s) merged\n`);
   232	  return state.status === LOOP_STATUS.DONE ? 0 : state.status === LOOP_STATUS.PARKED ? EXIT_PARKED : 0;
   233	}

     3	Status: APPROVED — Gate 1 passed at iteration 4 of 5 (2026-07-18,
     4	codex/gpt-5.5 @ xhigh, `degraded: model-fallback`).
     5	
     6	## Context
     7	
     8	baton v1 (Layer 1, cross-harness usage-limit failover) is complete: Gate-2
     9	passed, a 40-finding surface audit folded, and the live e2e handoff loop
    10	verified through the real installed plugin. Layers 2 and 3 automate the
    11	development methodology itself on top of that foundation: a goal-driven loop
    12	with review gates (Layer 2) and a dual-worktree adversarial write/review
    13	pipeline (Layer 3).
    14	
    15	## Locked decisions (Saksham, 2026-07-18)
    16	
    17	1. **Packaging**: inside the baton repo — new commands `baton loop` and
    18	   `baton pipeline`, sharing the role matrix, resolver, doctor, and
    19	   checkpoint/handoff machinery.
    20	2. **Layer 3 swap cadence**: per subtask — writer and reviewer swap seats
    21	   every subtask; the planner role plans and the merger role performs the
    22	   final adversarial merge (concrete chains live in `baton.config.json`).
    23	3. **Layer 2 smoke gate**: one staged gate after planning — the loop must
    24	   produce a minimal end-to-end slice (build + run + one real assertion) and
    25	   get explicit approval before the full run; the 5-iteration cap governs
    26	   every gate.
    27	4. **Driver**: a supervisor CLI — spawns headless role children
    28	   (`claude -p` / `codex exec`), parses verdicts, and invokes baton handoff
    29	   automatically on limit death. Survives the operator's session ending.
    30	
    31	## Design
    32	
    33	### Loop spec
    34	
    35	`loop.json` at repo root (schema `baton/loop@1`), scaffolded by
    36	`baton loop init "<goal>"`:
    37	
    38	- `goal`, `constraints[]` (falsifiable acceptance criteria — the loop's exit
    39	  condition), `smoke` ({cmd, expect} — the staged-gate slice definition),
    40	- `phases` — an ordered list of `{id, role}` entries: `id` is a free label
    41	  for progress display; `role` MUST be a matrix role ID (validation fails on
    42	  any unknown role before a child spawns). Default:
    43	  plan(`planner`) → gate-1(`plan-reviewer`) → smoke — itself a TDD mini-cycle
    44	  per the repo contract: smoke-tests(`test-author`) →
    45	  smoke-verify(`test-verifier`) → smoke-build(`implementer`) → the human
    46	  smoke gate — → per-subtask cycle (`test-author` → `test-verifier` →
    47	  `implementer` → `subtask-reviewer`) → gate-2(`final-reviewer-a`,
    48	  `final-reviewer-b`) → done,
    49	- `budgets` ({iterationCap: 5, perRoleTimeoutMin, maxChildrenPerPhase}),
    50	- roles resolve through the existing `baton.config.json` matrix — never
    51	  hardcoded models.
    52	
    53	### Role-matrix additions (Gate-1 iteration 1, finding 1)
    54	
    55	The loop references roles by ID only; every referenced role must exist in the
    56	matrix or `loop.json` validation fails (`unknown role '<id>'`, exit 2, before
    57	any child spawns). Four roles are ADDED to `baton.config.json` +
    58	`templates/baton.config.json.tpl` in the first implementation task:
    59	
    60	- `subtask-reviewer` — per-subtask diff review inside the loop cycle.
    61	- `merger` — the only role that writes main in pipeline mode; adversarial
    62	  re-check before merge.
    63	- `worker-a`, `worker-b` — the two pipeline seats. Subtask N assigns
    64	  writer = seat (N mod 2), reviewer = the other seat; the SEATS swap, and each
    65	  seat's concrete platform/model comes from its configured chain via the
    66	  resolver. No vendor or model name appears in this plan, child prompts, or
    67	  loop code — chains live in config only.
    68	
    69	### `baton loop` (Layer 2)
    70	
    71	- `baton loop init <goal>` — scaffold spec + plan template; idempotent,
    72	  dry-run-able, same two-phase plan/apply discipline as `baton init`.
    73	- `baton loop run [--approve-smoke <token>]` — the state machine. State lives
    74	  in gitignored `.handoff/loop/state.json` + `journal.ndjson` (same atomic
    75	  write + journal replay machinery as the bundle; loop position is also
    76	  checkpointed into the handoff bundle so a limit death carries it).
    77	
    78	### Root + ownership discipline (Gate-1 iteration 1, finding 2)
    79	
    80	One authoritative root: the MAIN repo root where `loop.json` lives. The
    81	supervisor is the SOLE writer of `.handoff/loop/` and of the main root's
    82	handoff bundle; every supervisor-driven baton invocation passes explicit
    83	`--root <mainRoot>` and a supervisor-owned stable session hint
    84	(`--session loop-<runId>` — a NEW flag added to `checkpoint`; `receive`
    85	already has it), so the bundle has exactly one stable owner.
    86	
    87	**Child-hook policy (Gate-1 iteration 2, finding 1; iteration 3,
    88	finding 1)** — children never touch OR read any bundle, by construction: the
    89	supervisor sets `BATON_SUPERVISED_CHILD=1` in every child's environment, and
    90	EVERY baton hook command — the hook entrypoints, `baton checkpoint`, and
    91	`baton session-start` (which would otherwise read the bundle and inject
    92	pending-handoff resume context into the child) — quietly no-ops (exit 0,
    93	empty output) when it is set. This covers
    94	Layer-2 children running in the MAIN root (where root discovery would
    95	otherwise find the supervisor-owned bundle and the foreign-session guard
    96	would reject their stable hints) and pipeline children in worktrees alike;
    97	narrative checkpointing for supervised work is the supervisor's job, driven
    98	by parsed child results. Contract tests: (a) Layer-2 — two concurrent
    99	main-root children with distinct stable session hints and live hooks →
   100	zero bundle mutations, zero foreign-session rejections, supervisor
   101	`--session` checkpoints land; (b) pipeline — same from two worktrees while
   102	the supervisor writes loop state.
   103	- Each role invocation is a headless child under the supervisor:
   104	  - claude-code roles: `claude -p "<role prompt>" --permission-mode
   105	    acceptEdits --allowedTools ...` (allowlist per role: reviewers read-only).
   106	  - codex roles: `codex exec -C <root> -s <sandbox> --model <m> -c
   107	    model_reasoning_effort=<e> "<prompt>" </dev/null` (stdin closed — the
   108	    stdin-hang class is a known field failure).
   109	  - Verdict contract: children end with the structured tail
   110	    (`VERDICT: APPROVED|APPROVED_WITH_NOTES|BLOCKED` + FINDINGS) already used
   111	    by every review gate; the supervisor parses exactly that.
   112	
   113	### Child supervision contract (Gate-1 iteration 1, finding 7)
   114	
   115	`children.mjs` owns the full child lifecycle, not just argv assembly:
   116	
   117	- Spawn detached in its OWN process group; stdin closed at spawn (the
   118	  codex-exec stdin-hang class); per-role timeout → SIGTERM to the group, 10 s
   119	  grace, then SIGKILL to the group (no zombie grandchildren; reap verified in
   120	  tests with a child that forks a sleeper).
   121	- Full child output streams to `.handoff/loop/children/<child-id>.log` with a
   122	  byte cap (head preserved, middle truncated with a marker — the verdict tail
   123	  is at the END so the tail is never truncated).
   124	- **Verdict parsing is region-bounded (prompt-echo defense)**: only the
   125	  child's FINAL message region is parsed — claude-code children run with a
   126	  structured output format and the parser reads the result field; codex
   127	  children are parsed strictly AFTER the last `tokens used` marker. The
   128	  prompt region (which quotes `VERDICT:` in its instructions) is never
   129	  scanned. Multiple tails in the final region → the LAST one wins.
   130	- An unparseable, absent, or truncated verdict is BLOCKED-with-escalation,
   131	  never APPROVED; a child that exits after making edits but before a verdict
   132	  parks the subtask with its worktree preserved for inspection.
   133	- A generic nonzero exit classifies as `other-error` (existing classifier
   134	  rule) → the loop retries once, then parks; it NEVER triggers limit failover
   135	  without limit-specific evidence.
   136	- Fixture-driven tests: prompt echo, multiple verdict tails, truncated
   137	  transcript, timeout kill/reap, zombie grandchild, non-limit nonzero exit.
   138	
   139	### Supervisor lifetime and recovery (Gate-1 iteration 2, finding 5)
   140	
   141	"Survives the operator's session ending" is a defined mechanism, not a hope:
   142	
   143	- `baton loop run` is FOREGROUND by default. `--detach` re-launches the
   144	  supervisor in its own session (`setsid`), output to
   145	  `.handoff/loop/supervisor.log`, and returns.
   146	- A run lock `.handoff/loop/supervisor.lock` carries {pid, process
   147	  start-time, host, runId} — the same provably-dead machinery as the
   148	  Layer-1 bundle lock. A second `loop run` against a LIVE supervisor is
   149	  refused; against a provably-dead one it recovers.
   150	- The state machine is journal-replayable: recovery replays
   151	  `journal.ndjson`, marks any in-flight child of the dead supervisor as
   152	  interrupted (its process group is gone or killed on adoption), and either
   153	  continues from the last completed step or parks with an escalation note —
   154	  never re-runs a completed gate iteration.
   155	- Acceptance adds a supervisor-death test: kill the supervisor (and its
   156	  launching session) mid-subtask → a fresh `loop run` recovers via the
   157	  journal and continues or parks; no orphaned children survive (process
   158	  groups reaped on adoption).
   159	
   160	- **Smoke gate (staleness-proof — Gate-1 iteration 1, finding 8; TDD order —
   161	  iteration 2, finding 2)**: after gate-1, the smoke slice is built as a TDD
   162	  mini-cycle (test-author writes the failing smoke assertion → test-verifier
   163	  approves it → implementer writes only enough to pass it — the repo TDD
   164	  contract applies to the slice, not just to subtasks); the supervisor then
   165	  runs `smoke.cmd`, records output, and STOPS, writing
   166	  `.handoff/loop/SMOKE-REVIEW.md` plus `smoke-approval.json` containing an
   167	  approval token = digest over {loop-state digest, smoke.cmd, output digest,
   168	  HEAD, content-sensitive git digest (existing snapshot machinery), child
   169	  assignment}. Resuming requires `baton loop run --approve-smoke <token>`;
   170	  the supervisor recomputes the digest first and REFUSES a drifted token
   171	  (anything changed since the human looked) — it parks for a fresh smoke run
   172	  instead. No full-run budget is spent before a valid approval.
   173	- **Limit failover (the baton integration — Gate-1 iteration 1, finding 3)**:
   174	  an exact transaction, not a loose chain. On child death: (1) reap the
   175	  process group and freeze the transcript; (2) classify via `baton detect`;
   176	  non-limit → the retry/park path above. On a limit classification:
   177	  (3) checkpoint the loop position INTO THE BUNDLE (supervisor
   178	  `checkpoint --session loop-<runId>`) so the seal carries it and the receipt
   179	  token binds it; (4) seal via `finalize --reason-class usage-limit --root
   180	  <mainRoot>` (an already-dead/unsealed bundle takes the degraded-open path
   181	  receive already supports); (5) refresh probes; (6) `receive --prepare` then
   182	  `--commit` back-to-back with IDENTICAL intake flags, the supervisor session
   183	  hint, and the same explicit root, with NO intervening writes; (7) resolver
   184	  output (with the dead entry avoided) picks the relaunch platform+model;
   185	  (8) relaunch the child with the resume prompt. A stale-token rejection
   186	  triggers ONE automatic re-prepare/commit retry; a second failure parks.
   187	  Between prepare and commit the failover holds the loop transaction lock and
   188	  writes NOTHING — `.handoff/loop/` writes are not token-bound inputs, so
   189	  this is enforced by construction and ASSERTED in tests (fs spy: zero
   190	  `.handoff/loop/` writes inside the window), not assumed. Drift-rejection
   191	  tests inject the inputs the token actually binds: git change and probe
   192	  refresh between prepare and commit → stale-token → one retry path. Plus:
   193	  open-bundle (unsealed) limit death; the full seal→receive→relaunch happy
   194	  path.
   195	- **Model-level failover (Gate-1 iteration 1, finding 4)**: the resolver
   196	  gains entry-level avoidance — `avoidEntries: [{platform, model}]` alongside
   197	  the existing platform-level `avoid[]` — and the classifier gains a
   198	  `model-unavailable` signature class (distinct from usage-limit; seeded with
   199	  the verified account-tier rejection string). A model rejected at spawn adds
   200	  its {platform, model} entry to avoidEntries and re-resolves: other models
   201	  on the SAME platform remain selectable. Named test: first chain entry
   202	  rejected as model-unavailable → the next entry on the same platform is
   203	  selected, with no platform-wide avoidance.
   204	  All platforms/entries exhausted → the loop parks with a resume-at hint
   205	  instead of dying.
   206	- **Caps**: every gate loops at most 5 iterations; on exhaustion the loop
   207	  parks and escalates (writes `.handoff/loop/ESCALATION.md`, notifies) — never
   208	  a 6th run.
   209	
   210	### `baton pipeline` (Layer 3 — a loop preset)
   211	
   212	- `baton pipeline run` = `loop run` with the dual-worktree subtask cycle:
   213	  - Two persistent worktrees `.worktrees/wt-a`, `.worktrees/wt-b`
   214	    (`git worktree add`); `pipeline init` ensure-lines `.worktrees/` into
   215	    `.gitignore` (same idempotent machinery as the `.handoff/` line).
   216	  - Subtask N: the writer seat implements on a namespaced branch
   217	    (`baton/wt-<seat>/subtask-N`) in its worktree; the reviewer seat reviews
   218	    the diff from the OTHER worktree (fresh checkout, fresh context). Seats
   219	    swap every subtask: worker-a writes / worker-b reviews → worker-b writes /
   220	    worker-a reviews → … (concrete models per seat come from the role
   221	    matrix — see Role-matrix additions).
   222	  - Verdict flow: APPROVED → the merger role adversarially re-checks and
   223	    merges into main (the ONLY writer to main); BLOCKED → writer fixes,
   224	    ≤5 iterations, then escalate. After each merge both worktrees
   225	    fast-forward.
   226	
   227	  **Worktree transaction layer (Gate-1 iteration 1, finding 5)** —
   228	  `worktrees.mjs` treats every git operation as a guarded transaction:
   229	  - Branch namespace `baton/wt-*/…` is loop-owned; anything else is never
   230	    touched.
   231	  - Preflight before each child: worktree exists in `git worktree list`,
   232	    index clean, HEAD on the expected branch — a dirty or drifted worktree
   233	    parks the subtask instead of spawning.
   234	  - Postflight after each child: HEAD still on the expected branch, main
   235	    unmoved by the child — violation parks with the evidence.
   236	  - Merges run under a supervisor-held merge lock; worktree sync after merge
   237	    is ff-only (a non-ff sync is a corruption signal → park); merge conflicts
   238	    park + escalate, never auto-resolved.
   239	  - Stale metadata self-heals: a raw-deleted worktree directory is detected
   240	    (`git worktree list` vs fs), pruned (`git worktree prune`), and re-added;
   241	    teardown uses `git worktree remove` + branch deletion so acceptance
   242	    constraint 5 (no residue) holds.
   243	  - Adversarial tests: stale branch from a previous run, dirty worker,
   244	    main moved mid-subtask, merge conflict, raw-deleted `.worktrees/`,
   245	    untracked `.worktrees/` (missing gitignore line → doctor check).
   246	
   247	  **Attribution enforcement for child commits (Gate-1 iteration 1,
   248	  finding 6)** — the invariant is enforced, not assumed:
   249	  - The supervisor sets `GIT_AUTHOR_NAME/EMAIL` and `GIT_COMMITTER_NAME/EMAIL`
   250	    (sole author identity) in EVERY child's environment.
   251	  - Every merge is gated on an attribution scan over the exact range
   252	    `main..baton/wt-<seat>/subtask-N` — author/committer identity plus the
   253	    forbidden-trailer patterns (the existing doctor patterns, applied to the
   254	    full range, not a last-50 window). A violation blocks the merge and
   255	    parks.
   256	  - The existing doctor 50-commit scan remains the repo-wide backstop.
   257	
   258	### Module layout (all under `core/src/loop/`)
   259	
   260	`spec.mjs` (load/validate loop.json incl. role-existence check) · `state.mjs`
   261	(state machine + journal + smoke-approval token) · `children.mjs` (full child
   262	lifecycle: spawn/process-group/timeout/log-cap, region-bounded verdict parse,
   263	detect-on-exit) · `failover.mjs` (the limit-death transaction) ·
   264	`worktrees.mjs` (guarded git transactions, branch discipline, attribution
   265	range scan) · `commands/loop.mjs`, `commands/pipeline.mjs`. Plus three Layer-1
   266	extensions: `roles/resolve.mjs` gains `avoidEntries[]` (entry-level
   267	avoidance, backward-compatible — omitted means current behavior); the
   268	signature table gains the `model-unavailable` class; `checkpoint` gains a
   269	`--session <hint>` flag (supervisor identity), and the
   270	`BATON_SUPERVISED_CHILD` no-op guard lands in the hook entrypoints,
   271	`checkpoint`, AND `session-start` (every hook-invoked command).
   272	
   273	### Acceptance constraints (the loop's exit condition)
   274	
   275	1. On a fixture repo, `baton loop run` drives a toy goal end-to-end headlessly:
   276	   plan → gate-1 → smoke slice AS A TDD MINI-CYCLE (failing smoke assertion
   277	   authored and verifier-approved BEFORE the slice implementation — order
   278	   asserted from the journal) → parked for approval → (approved) → 2 subtasks
   279	   TDD → gate-2 → done, with review artifacts under `reviews/` in the fixture.
   280	   Layer-2 children run in the main root with live hooks and neither mutate
   281	   NOR read the supervisor-owned bundle: under `BATON_SUPERVISED_CHILD=1`,
   282	   hook commands on all three platforms produce zero bundle writes AND zero
   283	   session-start resume context (both asserted).
   284	2. Simulated limit death (child exits with a verbatim limit banner) mid-subtask
   285	   → the loop seals, remaps the role off the dead platform, resumes on the
   286	   other harness, and completes the subtask. Chained A→B→A tested. Separately:
   287	   a model-unavailable rejection at spawn selects the NEXT entry on the same
   288	   platform (no platform-wide avoidance).
   289	3. `baton pipeline run` completes 2 subtasks with alternating writer/reviewer
   290	   across the two worktrees; the attribution range scan passes for main AND
   291	   both worker branch ranges (child commits included) — sole author, zero AI
   292	   attribution; reviewer children run read-only.
   293	4. The 5-cap parks + escalates on every gate (unit-forced exhaustion test);
   294	   no gate ever runs a 6th iteration. Killing the supervisor (and its
   295	   launching session) mid-subtask → a fresh `loop run` recovers via the
   296	   journal and continues or parks, with no orphaned children.
   297	5. Deleting `.handoff/` and `.worktrees/` leaves the fixture repo exactly as
   298	   a plain git repo (no residue outside gitignored trees).
   299	6. All existing 878 tests stay green; the new suite covers spec validation,
   300	   state-machine transitions (unit, injected io), child verdict parsing
   301	   (fixture transcripts), failover (fake children), and worktree discipline.
   302	
   303	### Top risks
   304	
   305	1. **Headless permission surfaces** — Claude Code children need permission
   306	   flags that stay inside the sandboxed repo; reviewers get read-only
   307	   allowlists. Mitigation: per-role allowlist table + spike task 0.
   308	2. **Verdict-parse drift** — models sometimes wrap the tail; the
   309	   region-bounded parser (Child supervision contract) takes the LAST tail in
   310	   the final-message region only and treats an unparseable one as
   311	   BLOCKED-with-escalation, never as APPROVED.
   312	3. **Cost runaway** — per-phase child caps + iteration caps + the smoke gate
   313	   before any full-run spend; every child's token/duration logged to the loop
   314	   journal.
   315	4. **Worktree state divergence** — only the merger writes main; workers are
   316	   branch-jailed; ff-sync after each merge; `baton pipeline doctor` checks
   317	   worktree health.
   318	5. **Cross-platform child API drift** — the spawn contract lives in ONE module
   319	   (children.mjs) with per-platform fixtures, same seam discipline as
   320	   normalize.mjs.
   321	
   322	## Out of scope for this milestone
   323	
   324	Multi-repo loops; parallel subtask fan-out (>1 writer at once); cloud/cron
   325	driving (the supervisor is local-first; scheduled runs are a later layer);
   326	LLM-judged smoke verdicts (the smoke gate is a human gate by decision 3).
   327

    27	/**
    28	 * Repo-root discovery (plan §Root discovery): `--root` wins; else the nearest
    29	 * ancestor of io.cwd carrying `.handoff/` or `baton.config.json`; else the
    30	 * nearest ancestor carrying `.git` (the repo toplevel — and a boundary the
    31	 * walk never crosses, so a nested repo resolves to itself); else io.cwd.
    32	 * @param {any} io @param {Record<string, string | boolean>} flags
    33	 * @returns {string}
    34	 */
    35	export function resolveRoot(io, flags) {
    36	  if (typeof flags.root === 'string' && flags.root.length > 0) return flags.root;
    37	  // Normalize separators up front (gate-2 iter-2 M2): a Windows cwd arrives
    38	  // backslash-separated, but Node fs accepts forward slashes, so the ascent
    39	  // and the marker checks work in one forward-slash space. The last separator
    40	  // is either kind; the loop stops at a drive/UNC or filesystem root.
    41	  const start = normSep(io.cwd);
    42	  let dir = start;
    43	  while (true) {
    44	    try {
    45	      if (io.fs.existsSync(joinNorm(dir, '.handoff')) || io.fs.existsSync(joinNorm(dir, 'baton.config.json'))) return dir;
    46	      if (io.fs.existsSync(joinNorm(dir, '.git'))) return dir; // repo toplevel; never walk past a .git boundary
    47	    } catch {
    48	      break;
    49	    }
    50	    const cut = dir.lastIndexOf('/');
    51	    if (cut < 0) break;
    52	    const parent = cut === 0 ? '/' : dir.slice(0, cut);
    53	    // At a drive-relative ceiling ("C:") examine the drive ROOT ("C:/") once —
    54	    // a repo can live directly at C:/ (iter-3 F6) — then stop.
    55	    if (/^[A-Za-z]:$/.test(parent)) {
    56	      const driveRoot = `${parent}/`;
    57	      if (driveRoot !== dir) {
    58	        dir = driveRoot;
    59	        continue;
    60	      }
    61	      break;
    62	    }
    63	    if (parent === dir) break;
    64	    dir = parent;
    65	  }
    66	  return start;
    67	}

    78	export function isSupervisedChild(io) {

    86	/**
    87	 * Validate a platform id against the enum (surface-audit fold): a typo'd

    89	 * rejected the REAL platform's checkpoints as foreign.
    90	 * @param {string | boolean | undefined} value

    92	 */
    93	export function platformError(value) {
    94	  if (value === undefined) return null;
    95	  if (typeof value === 'string' && PLATFORMS.includes(value)) return null;
    96	  return `platform must be one of ${PLATFORMS.join('|')} (got ${JSON.stringify(value)})`;

    99	/**
   100	 * Strict flag parser (surface-audit fold; docs/design/core.md §tooling always
   101	 * specified strict parsing). spec maps each flag name to 'string' | 'boolean';
   102	 * `json` and `root` are implied common flags. Errors — unknown flag, stray
   103	 * positional, missing string-flag value — return {error} so the command exits
   104	 * 2 instead of silently losing intent: the lenient parser let a stray token
   105	 * flip SAFETY flags off (checkpoint/doctor --strict, remap --native-only,
   106	 * receive --print-prompt all read fail-dangerous exit 0).
   107	 * @param {string[]} args
   108	 * @param {Record<string, 'string' | 'boolean'>} spec
   109	 * @returns {{flags: Record<string, string | boolean>, error?: string}}
   110	 */
   111	export function parseFlagsStrict(args, spec) {
   112	  /** @type {Record<string, 'string' | 'boolean'>} */
   113	  const full = { json: 'boolean', root: 'string', ...spec };
   114	  /** @type {Record<string, string | boolean>} */
   115	  const flags = {};
   116	  for (let i = 0; i < args.length; i += 1) {
   117	    const a = args[i];
   118	    if (!a.startsWith('--')) return { flags, error: `unexpected argument '${a}' — flags are --name [value]` };
   119	    const key = a.slice(2);
   120	    const kind = full[key];
   121	    if (kind === undefined) return { flags, error: `unknown flag --${key}` };
   122	    if (kind === 'boolean') {
   123	      // A following token that is not a flag would have been this flag's
   124	      // silent "value" under the lenient parser; reject it loudly.
   125	      const next = args[i + 1];
   126	      if (next !== undefined && !next.startsWith('--')) {
   127	        return { flags, error: `unexpected token '${next}' after --${key} (a bare flag takes no value)` };
   128	      }
   129	      flags[key] = true;
   130	      continue;
   131	    }
   132	    const next = args[i + 1];
   133	    // A string flag consumes the next token unless it is itself a KNOWN flag
   134	    // (so `--commit --origin x` errors instead of eating '--origin').
   135	    if (next === undefined || (next.startsWith('--') && full[next.slice(2)] !== undefined)) {
   136	      return { flags, error: `--${key} requires a value` };
   137	    }
   138	    flags[key] = next;
   139	    i += 1;
   140	  }
   141	  return { flags };
   142	}
   143	
   144	/**
   145	 * Minimal flag parser: `--key value` pairs (value = next token not starting
   146	 * with --), bare `--key` booleans, positionals collected in order.
   147	 * @param {string[]} args
   148	 * @returns {{flags: Record<string, string | boolean>, positionals: string[]}}
   149	 */
   150	export function parseFlags(args) {
   151	  /** @type {Record<string, string | boolean>} */
   152	  const flags = {};
   153	  /** @type {string[]} */
   154	  const positionals = [];
   155	  for (let i = 0; i < args.length; i += 1) {
   156	    const a = args[i];
   157	    if (a.startsWith('--')) {
   158	      const key = a.slice(2);
   159	      const next = args[i + 1];
   160	      if (next !== undefined && !next.startsWith('--')) {
   161	        flags[key] = next;
   162	        i += 1;
   163	      } else {
   164	        flags[key] = true;
   165	      }
   166	    } else {
   167	      positionals.push(a);
   168	    }
   169	  }
   170	  return { flags, positionals };
   171	}

    24	/**
    25	 * Opt-in transcript-tail capture (gate-2 fix 10). Returns the bounded,
    26	 * redacted tail or null when the gate is closed: config off (the default),
    27	 * not a PreCompact payload, no usable transcript_path, or an unreadable /
    28	 * symlinked file. The path comes from an untrusted hook payload — it is read
    29	 * only under explicit opt-in, must lstat as a regular file, and its content
    30	 * passes the secret-redaction filter before storage.
    31	 * @param {string} root @param {any} raw parsed hook payload @param {any} io
    32	 * @returns {string | null}
    33	 */
    34	function captureTranscriptTail(root, raw, io) {
    35	  const cfg = safeReadJson(io.fs, `${root}/baton.config.json`);
    36	  if (!cfg.ok || cfg.value?.capture?.transcriptTail !== true) return null;
    37	
    38	  const eventName = raw && typeof raw === 'object' ? (raw.hook_event_name ?? raw.event) : null;
    39	  if (typeof eventName !== 'string' || !/precompact/i.test(eventName)) return null;
    40	  const path = raw.transcript_path;
    41	  if (typeof path !== 'string' || path.length === 0) return null;
    42

    44	    if (!io.fs.lstatSync(path).isFile()) return null;
    45	    // Allowlist the transcript path (gate-2 iter-2 m1): the payload is
    46	    // untrusted, so its resolved realpath must sit under the repo root, the
    47	    // user's ~/.claude tree (where Claude Code actually writes transcripts),

    49	    const fileReal = io.fs.realpathSync(path);
    50	    // On POSIX a backslash is a LEGAL filename character, not a separator, so
    51	    // normalizing '\\'→'/' in the containment check (isContained) could make an
    52	    // out-of-tree sibling like '/repo\\evil/f' appear contained (iter-4 I9).
    53	    // The transcript path is the one fully-untrusted path that reaches
    54	    // containment, so refuse a backslash-bearing realpath off Windows.
    55	    if (io.platform !== 'win32' && String(fileReal).includes('\\')) return null;

    57	    const allowed = [root];

    59	    if (typeof home === 'string' && home.length > 0) allowed.push(`${home}/.claude`);
    60	    const dir = cfg.value?.capture?.transcriptDir;
    61	    if (typeof dir === 'string' && dir.length > 0) allowed.push(dir);
    62	    const under = (/** @type {string} */ base) => {
    63	      let baseReal;

    65	        baseReal = io.fs.realpathSync(base);
    66	      } catch {
    67	        baseReal = base;
    68	      }

    70	      // backslash realpaths never satisfy a forward-slash containment, so
    71	      // opt-in capture would silently never fire.
    72	      return isContained(baseReal, fileReal);
    73	    };

    80	      tail = Buffer.from(tail).subarray(-TAIL_BYTES).toString('utf8');

    83	  } catch {

    85	  }

    88	/** Event-shaped: an object carrying a string `type`. */
    89	const isEventShaped = (/** @type {any} */ v) => v !== null && typeof v === 'object' && !Array.isArray(v) && typeof v.type === 'string';
    90	
    91	/**
    92	 * Tolerant stdin parse (live /baton:handoff failure: a model sent one event
    93	 * per line and the whole narrative checkpoint silently failed). Accept the
    94	 * canonical single JSON value, a bare array of events, or NDJSON where every
    95	 * line is an event object or a {schema, events} wrapper. All-or-nothing: one
    96	 * non-event element fails the whole parse — never a partial apply.
    97	 * @param {string} text @returns {{ok: true, raw: any} | {ok: false, why?: string}}
    98	 */
    99	function parseStdin(text) {
   100	  let single;
   101	  let singleOk = false;
   102	  try {
   103	    single = JSON.parse(text);
   104	    singleOk = true;
   105	  } catch {
   106	    singleOk = false;
   107	  }
   108	  if (singleOk) {
   109	    if (Array.isArray(single)) {
   110	      return single.every(isEventShaped) ? { ok: true, raw: { schema: 'baton/event@1', events: single } } : { ok: false };
   111	    }
   112	    // A closest-miss of the documented wrapper — an events array whose schema
   113	    // key is missing or wrong — must ERROR, never silently degrade to a junk
   114	    // note with ok:true (the model cannot self-correct from a success
   115	    // envelope while its narrative was discarded).
   116	    if (single !== null && typeof single === 'object' && Array.isArray(single.events) && single.schema !== 'baton/event@1') {
   117	      return { ok: false, why: `stdin carries an events array but schema is ${single.schema === undefined ? 'missing' : JSON.stringify(single.schema)} — use {"schema":"baton/event@1","events":[…]}` };
   118	    }
   119	    // A single bare event object (string `type`, none of the raw-hook payload
   120	    // markers) is one of the two most likely readings of "pipe events on
   121	    // stdin" — accept it as that event instead of degrading it to a junk note.
   122	    if (
   123	      isEventShaped(single) &&
   124	      single.schema === undefined &&
   125	      !('hook_event_name' in single) &&
   126	      !('session_id' in single) &&
   127	      !('event' in single)
   128	    ) {
   129	      return { ok: true, raw: { schema: 'baton/event@1', events: [single] } };
   130	    }
   131	    return { ok: true, raw: single };
   132	  }
   133	  const lines = text.split('\n').map((l) => l.trim()).filter((l) => l !== '');
   134	  if (lines.length === 0) return { ok: false };
   135	  /** @type {any[]} */
   136	  const events = [];
   137	  for (const line of lines) {
   138	    let v;
   139	    try {
   140	      v = JSON.parse(line);
   141	    } catch {
   142	      return { ok: false };
   143	    }
   144	    if (v && typeof v === 'object' && v.schema === 'baton/event@1' && Array.isArray(v.events)) {
   145	      if (!v.events.every(isEventShaped)) return { ok: false };
   146	      events.push(...v.events);
   147	    } else if (isEventShaped(v)) {
   148	      events.push(v);
   149	    } else {
   150	      return { ok: false };
   151	    }
   152	  }
   153	  return { ok: true, raw: { schema: 'baton/event@1', events } };
   154	}

   156	/**
   157	 * `baton checkpoint` — mechanical checkpoint from a hook payload on stdin.
   158	 * Hook-safety rule: exit 0 on every soft failure so a checkpoint can never
   159	 * break the host harness; `--strict` opts into hard failures (CI).
   160	 * @param {string[]} args @param {any} io
   161	 * @returns {Promise<number>}
   162	 */
   163	export async function cmdCheckpoint(args, io) {
   164	  if (isSupervisedChild(io)) return 0;
   165	  const parsed = parseFlagsStrict(args, { platform: 'string', model: 'string', trigger: 'string', debounce: 'string', session: 'string', strict: 'boolean', 'take-over': 'boolean' });
   166	  if (parsed.error !== undefined) return usageError(io, parsed.flags, 'checkpoint', parsed.error);
   167	  const flags = parsed.flags;
   168	  const strict = flags.strict === true;
   169	  const platform = typeof flags.platform === 'string' ? flags.platform : null;
   170	  if (!platform) return usageError(io, flags, 'checkpoint', '--platform <claude-code|codex|cursor> is required');
   171	  const pErr = platformError(platform);
   172	  if (pErr) return usageError(io, flags, 'checkpoint', pErr);
   173	  // A supervisor passing --session must never silently fall back to
   174	  // payload-derived identity: an empty value is a hard usage error.
   175	  if (flags.session === '') return usageError(io, flags, 'checkpoint', '--session must be a non-empty session hint (it cannot be empty)');
   176	
   177	  try {
   178	    return await run(flags, platform, io);
   179	  } catch (err) {
   180	    const msg = /** @type {any} */ (err)?.message ?? String(err);
   181	    if (flags.json) emitEnvelope(io, { ok: false, error: { code: 'checkpoint-failed', msg } });
   182	    io.stderr.write(`baton checkpoint: ${msg}\n`);
   183	    return strict ? 1 : 0;
   184	  }
   185	}
   186	
   187	/**
   188	 * @param {Record<string, string | boolean>} flags
   189	 * @param {string} platform
   190	 * @param {any} io
   191	 * @returns {Promise<number>}
   192	 */
   193	async function run(flags, platform, io) {
   194	  const strict = flags.strict === true;
   195	  const root = resolveRoot(io, flags);
   196	  const paths = bundlePaths(root);
   197	  // The --json contract (gate-2 fix 11): exactly one envelope on stdout for
   198	  // every exit path — the exit code stays governed by hook-safety.
   199	  const finish = (/** @type {any} */ env, /** @type {number} */ code) => {
   200	    if (flags.json) emitEnvelope(io, env);
   201	    return code;
   202	  };
   203	
   204	  const parsed = parseStdin(typeof io.stdin === 'string' ? io.stdin : '');
   205	  if (!parsed.ok) {
   206	    const why = parsed.why ?? 'send one JSON value ({"schema":"baton/event@1","events":[…]}), a JSON array of events, or NDJSON event lines';
   207	    io.stderr.write(`baton checkpoint: stdin could not be parsed — ${why}\n`);
   208	    return finish({ ok: false, error: { code: 'bad-stdin', msg: why } }, strict ? 1 : 0);
   209	  }
   210	  const raw = parsed.raw;
   211	
   212	  const session = { host: io.host, pid: io.pid, startTime: io.startTime };
   213	  // The hook command declares which event it is (--trigger), so event identity
   214	  // never depends on parsing each harness's payload shape (Cursor's stop payload
   215	  // names the event nowhere normalize could find it).
   216	  const explicitEvent = typeof flags.trigger === 'string' ? flags.trigger : undefined;
   217	  const events = normalizeHookPayload(raw, platform, session, explicitEvent);
   218	  if (events.length === 0) return finish({ ok: true, data: { events: 0, rewritten: false } }, 0);
   219	
   220	  // --session: the caller (the loop supervisor) checkpoints under its OWN
   221	  // stable identity — every event's hint is overridden, so ownership,
   222	  // adoption, foreign checks, and journal writerIds all key on the flag,
   223	  // never on whatever session id the piped payload happens to carry.
   224	  if (typeof flags.session === 'string') {
   225	    for (const ev of events) {
   226	      ev.sessionHint = flags.session;
   227	      ev.unstable = false;
   228	    }
   229	  }
   230	
   231	  // Opt-in transcript tail (gate-2 fix 10): rides the journal as its own
   232	  // IMPORTANT event so the snapshot rewrite below persists it, and purge can
   233	  // strip it from every retained copy by its property name.
   234	  const tail = captureTranscriptTail(root, raw, io);
   235	  if (tail !== null) {
   236	    events.push({
   237	      type: 'transcript.set',
   238	      payload: { transcript: { capturedAt: io.now(), tail } },
   239	      source: platform,
   240	      sessionHint: events[0]?.sessionHint,
   241	      unstable: events[0]?.unstable ?? true,
   242	    });
   243	  }
   244	
   245	  const { bundle, warnings, unsafe } = loadBundle(root, io);
   246	  for (const w of warnings) io.stderr.write(`baton checkpoint: ${w}\n`);
   247	
   248	  // An unsafe managed tree (symlinked .handoff — gate-2 fix 6) is NOT "no
   249	  // bundle yet": seeding here would write through the link. Refuse everything;
   250	  // hook-safety keeps the refusal soft outside --strict.
   251	  if (unsafe === true) {
   252	    io.stderr.write('baton checkpoint: managed tree failed the symlink/realpath jail — nothing written\n');
   253	    return finish({ ok: false, error: { code: 'unsafe-tree', msg: warnings[0] ?? 'managed tree failed the jail check' } }, strict ? 1 : 0);
   254	  }
   255	
   256	  // Opt-in debounce (gate-2 major 14; Cursor afterFileEdit fires per edit):
   257	  // at most one real checkpoint per window, tracked by a per-platform stamp.
   258	  // The read-and-set runs UNDER the repo lock (gate-2 iter-2 M5) so two
   259	  // simultaneous afterFileEdit hooks can't both observe no stamp and both
   260	  // checkpoint — exactly one wins the window.

    16	 * instead of full receive preparation (gate-2 fix 8). Inspects only
    17	 * pending-foreign state: a bundle that is sealed OR limit-hit AND from
    18	 * another platform yields a harness-shaped notice on stdout; every other state
    19	 * — own bundle, open non-limit bundle, no bundle, corrupt tree — is a quiet
    20	 * no-op. Soft paths ALWAYS exit 0 (a session-start notice must never break a
    21	 * session); a mis-parsed invocation is a broken hook manifest, which is loud
    22	 * (exit 2) so init/doctor drift gets noticed. With --json (surface-audit fold)
    23	 * the standard envelope wraps the outcome so machine consumers can tell "no
    24	 * pending handoff" from a swallowed failure: data = {pending, shaped}.
    25	 * @param {string[]} args @param {any} io
    26	 * @returns {Promise<number>}
    27	 */
    28	export async function cmdSessionStart(args, io) {
    29	  if (isSupervisedChild(io)) return 0;
    30	  const parsed = parseFlagsStrict(args, { platform: 'string' });
    31	  if (parsed.error !== undefined) return usageError(io, parsed.flags, 'session-start', parsed.error);
    32	  const flags = parsed.flags;
    33	  const finish = (/** @type {boolean} */ pending, /** @type {any} */ shaped) => {
    34	    if (flags.json === true) emitEnvelope(io, { ok: true, data: { pending, shaped } });
    35	    else if (shaped !== null) io.stdout.write(typeof shaped === 'string' ? `${shaped}\n` : JSON.stringify(shaped) + '\n');
    36	    return 0;
    37	  };
    38	  try {
    39	    const platform = typeof flags.platform === 'string' ? flags.platform : null;
    40	    if (!platform || platformError(platform) !== null) {
    41	      io.stderr.write('baton session-start: --platform <claude-code|codex|cursor> is required — emitting nothing\n');
    42	      return finish(false, null);
    43	    }
    44	
    45	    const root = resolveRoot(io, flags);
    46	    // Full load (journal replay included): an unsealed limit death may live
    47	    // only as a post-snapshot journal note. Load warnings are deliberately
    48	    // not surfaced — this is a notice path, not a diagnostics path. An UNSAFE
    49	    // managed tree (symlinked .handoff — threat model / F12) is a quiet no-op:
    50	    // never emit context derived from a tree that failed the jail.
    51	    const { bundle, unsafe } = loadBundle(root, io);
    52	    if (bundle === null || unsafe === true) return finish(false, null);
    53	
    54	    const origin = bundle?.origin?.platform;
    55	    const pending = bundle?.handoff?.status === 'sealed' || bundle?.handoff?.reasonClass === 'usage-limit';
    56	    const foreign = typeof origin === 'string' && origin !== platform;
    57	    if (!pending || !foreign) return finish(false, null);
    58	
    59	    const kind = bundle.handoff.status === 'sealed' ? 'sealed' : 'limit-hit before sealing';
    60	    // The bundle is untrusted input (threat model, iter-3 F12): allowlist the
    61	    // origin label — never interpolate a crafted origin string into injected
    62	    // session context.
    63	    const label = typeof origin === 'string' && platformError(origin) === null ? origin : 'another platform';
    64	    const msg = `A handoff bundle from ${label} is pending in .handoff/ (${kind}). ${RESUME_HINT[platform] ?? RESUME_HINT.codex}`;
    65	
    66	    if (platform === 'cursor') return finish(true, { additional_context: msg });
    67	    if (platform === 'claude-code') return finish(true, { hookSpecificOutput: { hookEventName: 'SessionStart', additionalContext: msg } });
    68	    return finish(true, msg);
    69	  } catch {
    70	    return finish(false, null);
    71	  }
    72	}

     6	import { spawn } from 'node:child_process';
     7	import { readFileSync } from 'node:fs';
     8	import * as nodeFs from 'node:fs';

    10	import { checkHandoffTree } from '../../../core/src/util/jail.mjs';

    12	/** @param {any} io */
    13	const batonBin = (io) => `${io.env?.CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT ?? '.'}/core/bin/baton.mjs`;
    14	
    15	/**
    16	 * execFile-shaped spawn that supports {input}: async execFile has no input
    17	 * option, and a child reading stdin would otherwise block forever (gate-2
    18	 * reviewer-b finding 1). The io contract for this adapter is therefore
    19	 * "promise execFile honoring opts.input as the child's stdin".
    20	 * @param {string} cmd @param {string[]} args @param {{cwd?: string, input?: string, timeout?: number}} [opts]
    21	 * @returns {Promise<{stdout: string, stderr: string}>}
    22	 */
    23	function execFileWithInput(cmd, args = [], opts = {}) {
    24	  return new Promise((resolve, reject) => {
    25	    const child = spawn(cmd, args, { cwd: opts.cwd, timeout: opts.timeout ?? 25_000 });
    26	    let stdout = '';
    27	    let stderr = '';
    28	    child.stdout.on('data', (d) => (stdout += d));
    29	    child.stderr.on('data', (d) => (stderr += d));
    30	    child.on('error', reject);
    31	    child.on('close', (code) => {
    32	      if (code === 0) return resolve({ stdout, stderr });
    33	      const e = Object.assign(new Error(`Command failed: ${cmd}`), { code, stdout, stderr });
    34	      reject(e);
    35	    });
    36	    // Fail-open (audit finding 21): a child that dies without draining a large
    37	    // stdin payload emits an out-of-band EPIPE 'error' on stdin that bypasses
    38	    // every try/catch and crashes the hook with a stack trace. Swallow it —
    39	    // the close handler still reports the child's real exit.
    40	    child.stdin?.on('error', () => {});
    41	    if (typeof opts.input === 'string' && child.stdin) child.stdin.write(opts.input);
    42	    child.stdin?.end();
    43	  });
    44	}
