    38	
    39	function version() {
    40	  const pkgPath = join(dirname(fileURLToPath(import.meta.url)), '..', '..', 'package.json');
    41	  return JSON.parse(readFileSync(pkgPath, 'utf8')).version;
    42	}
    43	
    44	const USAGE = `usage: baton <command> [options]
    45	
    46	commands:
    47	  ${COMMANDS.join(', ')}
    48	
    49	global options:
    50	  --version   print version
    51	  --json      machine-readable output: one compact JSON envelope on stdout
    52	`;
    53	
    54	/**
    55	 * The --json contract: exactly one compact envelope line on stdout, nothing else.
    56	 * @param {any} io
    57	 * @param {{ok: boolean, data?: any, warnings?: any[], error?: {code: string, msg: string} | null}} env
    58	 */
    59	function emitEnvelope(io, env) {
    60	  const full = { ok: env.ok, data: env.data ?? null, warnings: env.warnings ?? [], error: env.error ?? null };
    61	  io.stdout.write(JSON.stringify(full) + '\n');
    62	}
    63	
    64	/**
    65	 * @param {string[]} argv process args after the binary
    66	 * @param {any} io injected {cwd, stdout, stderr}
    67	 * @returns {number | Promise<number>} exit code
    68	 */
    69	export function run(argv, io) {
    70	  const args = argv.filter((a) => a !== '');
    71	  // Global --json intent is parsed BEFORE dispatch (gate-2 iter-2 M6): a
    72	  // top-level `--json` with no command, or --help/--version, must still emit
    73	  // exactly one envelope rather than raw text.
    74	  const jsonWanted = args.includes('--json');
    75	  const cmdIndex = args.findIndex((a) => !a.startsWith('--'));
    76	  const cmd = cmdIndex === -1 ? undefined : args[cmdIndex];
    77	
    78	  if (cmd === undefined) {
    79	    // No command — just flags (or nothing).
    80	    if (args.includes('--version')) {
    81	      if (jsonWanted) emitEnvelope(io, { ok: true, data: { version: version() } });
    82	      else io.stdout.write(version() + '\n');
    83	      return 0;
    84	    }
    85	    if (args.includes('--help')) {
    86	      if (jsonWanted) emitEnvelope(io, { ok: true, data: { usage: USAGE.trim() } });
    87	      else io.stdout.write(USAGE);
    88	      return 0;
    89	    }
    90	    // Bare invocation (or a lone --json): a usage error.
    91	    if (jsonWanted) emitEnvelope(io, { ok: false, error: { code: 'usage', msg: 'no command given' } });
    92	    else io.stdout.write(USAGE);
    93	    return 2;
    94	  }
    95	
    96	  // All flags (before and after the command token) reach the command; only the
    97	  // command token itself is stripped, so a leading `--json` isn't lost.
    98	  const rest = args.filter((_, i) => i !== cmdIndex);
    99	
   100	  // Usage errors honor the --json envelope contract too (gate-2 fix 11 + M6:
   101	  // global --json counts, wherever it sits).
   102	  const wantsJson = jsonWanted;
   103	  if (cmd === 'wrap') {
   104	    if (wantsJson) emitEnvelope(io, { ok: false, error: { code: 'usage', msg: 'baton wrap is reserved for a future release (PTY supervisor); not available in v1' } });
   105	    else io.stderr.write(`baton wrap is reserved for a future release (PTY supervisor); not available in v1.\n${USAGE}`);
   106	    return 2;
   107	  }
   108	  if (!COMMANDS.includes(cmd)) {
   109	    if (wantsJson) emitEnvelope(io, { ok: false, error: { code: 'usage', msg: `unknown command '${cmd}'` } });
   110	    else io.stderr.write(`baton: unknown command '${cmd}'\n${USAGE}`);
   111	    return 2;
   112	  }
   113	
   114	  if (rest.includes('--help')) {
   115	    // Subcommand help honors the global --json envelope too (iter-3 F8): with
   116	    // --json it must emit exactly one envelope, never raw usage text.
   117	    const usage = `usage: baton ${cmd} [options]\n\nSee README for the ${cmd} contract.`;
   118	    if (wantsJson) emitEnvelope(io, { ok: true, data: { usage } });
   119	    else io.stdout.write(`${usage}\n`);
   120	    return 0;
   121	  }
   122	
   123	  if (cmd === 'status') return cmdStatus(rest, io);
   124	  if (cmd === 'detect') return cmdDetect(rest, io);
   125	  if (cmd === 'remap') return cmdRemap(rest, io);
   126	  if (cmd === 'checkpoint') return cmdCheckpoint(rest, io);
   127	  if (cmd === 'finalize') return cmdFinalize(rest, io);
   128	  if (cmd === 'receive') return cmdReceive(rest, io);
   129	  if (cmd === 'init') return cmdInit(rest, io);
   130	  if (cmd === 'doctor') return cmdDoctor(rest, io);
   131	  if (cmd === 'purge-transcript') return cmdPurgeTranscript(rest, io);
   132	  if (cmd === 'session-start') return cmdSessionStart(rest, io);
   133	  if (cmd === 'recover') return cmdRecover(rest, io);
   134	  if (cmd === 'loop') return cmdLoop(rest, io);
   135	  if (cmd === 'pipeline') return cmdPipeline(rest, io);
   136	
   137	  const error = { code: 'not-implemented', msg: `baton ${cmd}: not implemented yet` };
   138	  if (rest.includes('--json')) {
   139	    emitEnvelope(io, { ok: false, error });
   140	  } else {
   141	    io.stderr.write(`${error.msg}\n`);
   142	  }
   143	  return 1;
   144	}

    35	/** @param {any} text @returns {string | null} */
    36	const parseVersion = (text) => String(text).match(/\d+(?:\.\d+)+/)?.[0] ?? null;
    37	
    38	/** Numeric segment-wise version compare: negative when a < b. */
    39	function cmpVersions(/** @type {string} */ a, /** @type {string} */ b) {
    40	  const as = a.split('.').map(Number);
    41	  const bs = b.split('.').map(Number);
    42	  for (let i = 0; i < Math.max(as.length, bs.length); i++) {
    43	    const d = (as[i] ?? 0) - (bs[i] ?? 0);
    44	    if (d !== 0) return d < 0 ? -1 : 1;
    45	  }
    46	  return 0;
    47	}

    49	/** @param {string} platform @param {any} io @returns {Promise<string | null>} */
    50	async function versionOf(platform, io) {
    51	  try {
    52	    const r = await io.execFile(VERSION_BINS[platform], ['--version'], { timeout: PROBE_TIMEOUT_MS });
    53	    return parseVersion(`${r.stdout ?? ''} ${r.stderr ?? ''}`);
    54	  } catch {
    55	    return null;
    56	  }
    57	}

    59	/** @param {string} platform @param {string | null} v @returns {{id: string, ok: boolean, detail: string}} */
    60	function versionCheck(platform, v) {
    61	  const id = `version-${platform}`;
    62	  if (v === null) {
    63	    return { id, ok: true, detail: `${VERSION_BINS[platform]} version unverifiable (not installed or --version unsupported) — floor not enforced` };
    64	  }
    65	  const f = FLOORS[platform];
    66	  if (f.floor && cmpVersions(v, f.floor) < 0) {
    67	    return { id, ok: false, detail: `${platform} ${v} is below the supported floor ${f.label} — lifecycle hooks unavailable; the adapter degrades to protocol-only (Tier C)` };
    68	  }
    69	  if (cmpVersions(v, TESTED[platform]) > 0) {
    70	    return { id, ok: true, detail: `${platform} ${v} is newer than tested (${TESTED[platform]}) — untested; signature drift is absorbed by the overlay table` };
    71	  }
    72	  return { id, ok: true, detail: `${platform} ${v} (tested ${TESTED[platform]})` };
    73	}

    75	/**
    76	 * Recursively collect every `command`/`commandWindows` STRING under a hook
    77	 * definition (codex nests hooks[].hooks[].command; cursor is [].command). Only
    78	 * real command fields — never matchers, comments, or serialized metadata — so
    79	 * enablement keys on an actual invocation (iter-5 A3).
    80	 * @param {any} def @returns {string[]}
    81	 */
    82	function hookCommands(def) {
    83	  /** @type {string[]} */
    84	  const out = [];
    85	  const walk = (/** @type {any} */ node) => {
    86	    if (Array.isArray(node)) {
    87	      for (const n of node) walk(n);
    88	    } else if (node && typeof node === 'object') {
    89	      if (typeof node.command === 'string') out.push(node.command);
    90	      if (typeof node.commandWindows === 'string') out.push(node.commandWindows);
    91	      if (Array.isArray(node.hooks)) walk(node.hooks);
    92	    }
    93	  };
    94	  walk(def);
    95	  return out;
    96	}

    98	/**
    99	 * Codex/Cursor hook surface: installed / enabled / trusted / observed-executing.
   100	 * `installed` = the hooks file references baton at all; `enabled` = the
   101	 * mechanical-checkpoint hook (the `checkpointEvent` key: codex `Stop`, cursor
   102	 * `stop`) declares a `baton checkpoint` COMMAND (iter-5 A3); `trusted` is not
   103	 * externally verifiable → 'unknown'; `observed` = a platform-sourced checkpoint
   104	 * whose payload.trigger is the checkpointEvent (the per-turn Stop canary). The
   105	 * ≤1-turn fidelity claim is gated on enabled AND observed.
   106	 * @param {any} io @param {string} root @param {any} p bundlePaths
   107	 * @param {string} platform @param {string} relPath @param {string} checkpointEvent @param {string} trustNote
   108	 */
   109	function hookSurfaceCheck(io, root, p, platform, relPath, checkpointEvent, trustNote) {
   110	  const id = `${platform}-hooks`;
   111	  let text = null;
   112	  try {
   113	    text = io.fs.readFileSync(`${root}/${relPath}`, 'utf8');
   114	  } catch {
   115	    text = null;
   116	  }
   117	  const installed = text !== null && text.includes('baton');
   118	  if (!installed) {
   119	    return {
   120	      id,
   121	      ok: true,
   122	      states: { installed: false, enabled: false, trusted: 'unknown', observed: false },
   123	      detail: `not installed — run 'baton init --${platform}' to write ${relPath}`,
   124	    };
   125	  }
   126	  // enabled: the checkpoint hook itself is declared with a `baton checkpoint`
   127	  // COMMAND (iter-5 A3) — inspect the actual command/commandWindows fields, not
   128	  // the serialized blob (a matcher/comment mentioning baton must not count). A
   129	  // file that references baton only in a session-start hook is installed but NOT
   130	  // checkpoint-enabled.
   131	  let enabled = false;
   132	  try {
   133	    const cfg = JSON.parse(/** @type {string} */ (text));
   134	    // Match the bare `baton checkpoint` form AND the absolute
   135	    // `"<node>" "<…/baton.mjs>" checkpoint` form init now writes (the entry
   136	    // script may be quote-terminated: `baton.mjs" checkpoint`).
   137	    enabled = hookCommands(cfg?.hooks?.[checkpointEvent]).some((c) => /\bbaton(?:\.mjs)?["']?\s+checkpoint\b/.test(c));
   138	  } catch {
   139	    enabled = false;
   140	  }
   141	  // observed: a mechanical checkpoint from THIS platform's CHECKPOINT hook is
   142	  // recorded — the journal entry must be platform-sourced AND carry
   143	  // payload.trigger === checkpointEvent (iter-5 A3). normalize stamps the hook
   144	  // event name as payload.trigger, so a SessionStart/PreCompact/triggerless
   145	  // entry no longer masquerades as the per-turn Stop canary.
   146	  let observed = false;
   147	  try {
   148	    for (const e of readAllTolerant(io.fs, p.journal).entries) {
   149	      const mine = String(e?.writerId ?? '').startsWith(`${platform}-`) || e?.source === platform;
   150	      if (mine && e?.payload?.trigger === checkpointEvent) {
   151	        observed = true;
   152	        break;
   153	      }
   154	    }
   155	  } catch {
   156	    observed = false;
   157	  }
   158	  const states = { installed: true, enabled, trusted: 'unknown', observed };
   159	  // The ≤1-turn claim needs the checkpoint hook DECLARED (enabled) AND OBSERVED —
   160	  // neither alone suffices.
   161	  const fidelityHolds = enabled && observed;
   162	  const enabledNote = enabled ? `${checkpointEvent} checkpoint hook enabled` : `${checkpointEvent} checkpoint hook NOT declared (enabled=false)`;
   163	  return {
   164	    id,
   165	    ok: true,
   166	    states,
   167	    detail: fidelityHolds
   168	      ? `installed, ${enabledNote}; trusted=unknown (not externally verifiable); execution OBSERVED (canary) — the ≤1-turn mechanical-staleness claim holds`
   169	      : `installed, ${enabledNote}; trusted=unknown — ${trustNote}; ${observed ? 'execution observed but' : 'execution not yet observed —'} the ≤1-turn fidelity claim is gated on the ${checkpointEvent} canary + enablement`,
   170	  };
   171	}

   175	// --version` proves the binary is INSTALLED, not that the server was reached or
   176	// that auth is valid, so its capability is capped at "installed". The codex/

   192	/** @param {any} io @param {string} path @returns {string | null} */
   193	function readOrNull(io, path) {
   194	  try {
   195	    return io.fs.readFileSync(path, 'utf8');

   197	    return null;
   198	  }
   199	}
   200	
   201	/**
   202	 * Classify one probe invocation per the two-dimension record: capability is
   203	 * the ordered ladder verified as far as the evidence goes; outcome is what the
   204	 * probe itself did. A server-issued limit response proves authentication.
   205	 * @param {{platform: string, bin: string, args: string[], capOnSuccess?: string}} probe @param {any} io @param {any} table
   206	 */
   207	async function runProbe(probe, io, table) {
   208	  try {
   209	    await io.execFile(probe.bin, probe.args, { timeout: PROBE_TIMEOUT_MS });
   210	    return { platform: probe.platform, capability: probe.capOnSuccess ?? 'reachable', outcome: 'ok' };
   211	  } catch (err) {
   212	    const e = /** @type {any} */ (err);
   213	    if (e?.code === 'ENOENT') return { platform: probe.platform, capability: null, outcome: 'error' };
   214	    if (e?.killed === true) return { platform: probe.platform, capability: 'installed', outcome: 'timeout' };
   215	    const text = `${e?.stdout ?? ''}\n${e?.stderr ?? ''}`;
   216	    if (table && classify({ text, exitCode: 1, platform: probe.platform, table, structured: null }).class === 'usage-limit') {
   217	      return { platform: probe.platform, capability: 'authenticated', outcome: 'rate-limited' };
   218	    }
   219	    if (/authenticated as|logged in as/i.test(text) && /unreachable|ECONN|offline|network/i.test(text)) {
   220	      return { platform: probe.platform, capability: 'authenticated', outcome: 'error' };
   221	    }
   222	    if (/not logged in|log ?in first|unauthorized|authenticat/i.test(text)) {
   223	      return { platform: probe.platform, capability: 'installed', outcome: 'error' };
   224	    }
   225	    return { platform: probe.platform, capability: 'installed', outcome: 'error' };
   226	  }
   227	}

   229	/** @param {any} io @param {string} root @returns {Promise<{id: string, ok: boolean, detail?: string}>} */
   230	async function gitGuardCheck(io, root) {
   231	  let log;
   232	  try {
   233	    log = await io.execFile('git', ['log', '-n', String(GUARD_COMMIT_BOUND), '--format=%h %s%n%n%b'], { cwd: root, timeout: 5000 });
   234	  } catch {
   235	    return { id: 'git-guard', ok: true, detail: 'git unavailable — commit scan skipped' };
   236	  }
   237	  // Chunks start at a line beginning with an abbreviated sha + space.
   238	  const chunks = log.stdout.split(/^(?=[0-9a-f]{7,40} )/m).filter((/** @type {string} */ c) => c.trim() !== '');
   239	  for (const chunk of chunks) {
   240	    if (TRAILER_PATTERNS.some((re) => re.test(chunk))) {
   241	      const sha = chunk.slice(0, chunk.indexOf(' '));
   242	      return {
   243	        id: 'git-guard',
   244	        ok: false,
   245	        detail: `commit ${sha} carries an AI attribution trailer — rewrite it before publishing (git rebase -i / git commit --amend)`,
   246	      };
   247	    }
   248	  }
   249	  return { id: 'git-guard', ok: true, detail: `no AI trailers in the last ${GUARD_COMMIT_BOUND} commits` };
   250	}

   260	export async function cmdDoctor(args, io) {

Verified OK: supervised-child no-op guards exist in `checkpoint`, `session-start`, and the Claude hook (`core/src/commands/checkpoint.mjs:164`, `core/src/commands/session-start.mjs:29`, `adapters/claude-code/scripts/hook.mjs:153`). Sole-author child env, merge-gate scan, and doctor backstop are present (`core/src/loop/children.mjs:21`, `core/src/loop/worktrees.mjs:140`, `core/src/commands/doctor.mjs:185`). But constraints 3 and 4 are not actually met, and constraint 1 is only nominal for real Claude children.

VERDICT: BLOCKED
FINDINGS:
1. [blocking] — Claude children are not rooted in the requested repo/worktree and do not enforce the resolved Claude model or structured JSON output; default `worker-b` is Claude, so pipeline subtask 2 can run from the supervisor cwd instead of `.worktrees/wt-b`, breaking “main merger-only” and making Claude verdict parsing depend on an unstated CLI default (`baton.config.json:14`, `core/src/commands/pipeline.mjs:138`, `core/src/loop/children.mjs:44`, `core/src/loop/children.mjs:51`, `core/src/loop/children.mjs:86`, `core/src/loop/children.mjs:93`, `core/src/loop/children.mjs:162`) — Fix: return `cwd: opts.root` for Claude children, pass the resolved model via the supported Claude flag, request the structured output format that `parseVerdict` expects, and add default-config pipeline coverage with Claude `worker-b`.
2. [blocking] — `pipeline run` has a path to a 6th attempt when `loop.json` sets `budgets.iterationCap > 5`: it bypasses `validateLoopSpec`, uses the raw cap, and only the reducer notices the hard 5-cap after another writer/reviewer pair has already spawned (`core/src/loop/spec.mjs:97`, `core/src/commands/pipeline.mjs:96`, `core/src/commands/pipeline.mjs:165`, `core/src/commands/pipeline.mjs:184`, `core/src/commands/pipeline.mjs:195`, `core/src/loop/state.mjs:90`) — Fix: validate the full loop spec or reject/clamp pipeline caps above 5 before setup/spawn; add a regression with `iterationCap: 6`.
3. [blocking] — Pipeline ignores writer child results, so a nonzero, unparseable, BLOCKED, usage-limit, or model-unavailable writer can still proceed to review and merge if later children approve (`core/src/commands/pipeline.mjs:184`, `core/src/commands/pipeline.mjs:186`, `core/src/commands/pipeline.mjs:195`, `core/src/commands/pipeline.mjs:222`, `core/src/loop/children.mjs:82`, `core/src/loop/children.mjs:200`) — Fix: inspect writer results before postflight/review; route BLOCKED through the retry cap, route classified failures through failover/park, and test failed writer cases.
4. [blocking] — Supervisor-death recovery does not adopt or reap in-flight detached children: the lock records only supervisor identity, `superviseChild` detaches the child process group, and recovery only reclaims a dead lock/replays state; the e2e test seeds a stale journal rather than killing a real supervisor with a live child (`core/src/commands/loop.mjs:142`, `core/src/commands/loop.mjs:162`, `core/src/loop/children.mjs:165`, `core/src/loop/children.mjs:179`, `tests/integration/loop-pipeline-e2e.test.mjs:292`) — Fix: journal child-start with pid/pgid before spawn, kill/adopt in-flight children when reclaiming a dead supervisor lock, then continue or park; add the real supervisor-death test promised by the plan.
5. [major] — Worktree guard composition is incomplete: `selfHealWorktree` is implemented but never imported/called by pipeline, listed-but-raw-deleted worktrees fall through to `git status` in a missing cwd, and reviewer seats spawn without a preflight despite the plan requiring preflight before each child (`core/src/loop/worktrees.mjs:79`, `core/src/loop/worktrees.mjs:103`, `core/src/loop/worktrees.mjs:217`, `core/src/commands/pipeline.mjs:21`, `core/src/commands/pipeline.mjs:189`, `docs/plans/2026-07-18-goal-loop-worktree-pipeline.md:231`) — Fix: call self-heal during setup/per-seat preflight, preflight reviewer seats too, catch git preflight errors and park.
6. [minor] — Deferred surfaces should be recorded as notes, not presented as implemented: `--detach` is in the approved plan but not accepted by `loop run`, probes are passed as `null`, and findings are local variables rather than persisted across supervisor restarts (`docs/plans/2026-07-18-goal-loop-worktree-pipeline.md:143`, `core/src/commands/loop.mjs:60`, `core/src/commands/loop.mjs:257`, `core/src/commands/pipeline.mjs:101`, `core/src/commands/loop.mjs:227`, `core/src/commands/pipeline.mjs:162`) — Fix: either implement or explicitly mark deferred; persist last findings in journal/state and integrate probe refresh/cache before claiming those contracts.
